## Assistant

## Done: signup → UUID → xyzfs (multi-user)

Character creation is **not** wired. Signup now mints a UUID and provisions a private xyzfs tree.

### Layout
```
00.login-signup/
├── users/<user_id>/profile.txt     # login name + uuid + xyzfs_path
└── xyzfs/
    ├── bin/
    └── users/
        └── <uuid>/                 # one tree per human
            ├── meta.txt
            ├── home/               # exchange/, net/
            └── projects/
```

### What happens on Create Account
1. Mint UUID (`/proc/sys/kernel/random/uuid`)
2. Write `users/<user_id>/profile.txt` with `uuid=` and `xyzfs_path=xyzfs/users/<uuid>`
3. Create `xyzfs/users/<uuid>/{home,projects,meta.txt}`
4. Auto-login → `current_login.txt` gets:
   - `current_user_id`
   - `current_user_uuid`
   - `current_xyzfs`

Many users = many sibling dirs under `xyzfs/users/`. Login name stays human-friendly; UUID is the fs key.

Legacy profiles without a UUID get one backfilled on first login.

### Harness
```bash
cd 0.user-pal👤️/00.login-signup
./test-harn-same/button.sh demo   # OVERALL: PASS
```
Covers two users with **distinct** UUIDs and coexisting xyzfs trees.

## Assistant

## Avatar creation = muchi-pals clone manager

Built under `0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/` as a **near-copy of the pets menu**, not a one-shot wizard.

### Flow
```
Main
 ├─ Faucet     → Claim Tokens (+10)
 ├─ Store      → Free starter (once) / Buy Clone (20 tokens)
 └─ Avatars    → list all clones
      ├─ Select → Sleep / Open Desktop Window / Customize DNA
      └─ Customize
           name, age (cli_io)
           cycle: gender, skin-tone face emoji (👨🏻…👨🏿 / 👩🏻…👩🏿),
                  hair, shirt, pants, height, weight
```

### Storage (multi-avatar, UUID-tagged)
| Layer | Path |
|--------|------|
| **Player fs (source of truth)** | `00.login-signup/xyzfs/users/<user_uuid>/home/avatars/<avatar_uuid>/` |
| Inventory | `…/home/avatars/inventory.txt` |
| Wallet | `…/home/wallet.txt` |
| **Local desktop pieces** | `01.avatar-creation/pieces/world_01/map_lobby/<avatar_uuid>/` (same contract as muchi-pals eggs) |

Each clone has its own **avatar UUID**. You can own many; DNA is re-editable anytime and stays in the player’s xyzfs.

### Desktop
`Open Desktop Window` forks `system/avatar_window` (muchi-pals `egg_window` copy) so clones can sit on the desktop like pets.

### How to run
```bash
# 1) log in (so clones land in your xyzfs)
cd 0.user-pal👤️/00.login-signup && ./button.sh run

# 2) clone factory
cd ../01.avatar-creation👤️
./button.sh compile
./button.sh run
# or headless mint:
./button.sh demo
```

### Smoke-tested
- Mint → local + xyzfs state written  
- Claim tokens → buy second clone  
- Two inventory entries, distinct UUIDs  
- DNA cycle (gender/skin/hair) updates both local and xyzfs  

### Intentionally later
- Auto-open this app right after signup  
- Full pet tick/feed/train parity  
- Shared economy with wsr-pal / pal-chain  
- Trading clones between users

## Assistant

Yes — building it out. The menus were dead for real reasons; that’s fixed and harness-proven.

## What was wrong

1. **`${piece_methods}` never loaded**  
   `active_target_id` stayed `main` on every screen, so faucet/store/avatars showed **[No Methods]** — buttons with no ops.

2. **Panel text was empty**  
   Layouts used `${panel_content}`, but compose only filled `game_map` / `view.txt`.

## What we fixed

| Fix | Effect |
|-----|--------|
| `write_chtpm_bridge()` (muchi pattern) | On each screen, set `active_target_id=faucet\|store\|avatars\|…` so METHODs appear |
| `panel_content` from `view.txt` | Status/messages/DNA show in the panel |
| DNA cycles on **Avatars detail** | Select a clone → Cycle Gender/Skin/Hair/… without a dead-end |
| `demo_menu_fx.sh` harness | Proves real FX end-to-end |

## Harness: **OVERALL: PASS**

- Claim tokens → wallet +10  
- Free starter → UUID piece + xyzfs inventory  
- Select clone → cycle skin emoji  
- Compose shows SELECTED + face  
- Open desktop window → `avatar_window` process  

## Play it

```bash
# 1) log in first (so xyzfs is yours)
cd 0.user-pal👤️/00.login-signup && ./button.sh run

# 2) clone factory
cd ../01.avatar-creation👤️
./button.sh compile
./button.sh run
```

**Path that has real FX now:**  
Main → **Faucet** (Claim Tokens) → **Store** (Free Starter) → **Avatars** (Select → cycle DNA / sleep / open window).

Optional proof without a terminal UI:

```bash
bash test-harn-same/scenarios/demo_menu_fx.sh
```

Next build-out options if you want to keep going: name/age apply on the detail screen, sprite rebuild on skin change, auto-launch avatar-creation after signup, or multi-window layout for several clones on the desktop.

find . -name 'window.pid' 2>/dev/null | while read -r f; do
  pid=$(cat "$f" 2>/dev/null | tr -d ' \n')
  if [ -z "$pid" ] || ! kill -0 "$pid" 2>/dev/null; then
    echo "removing stale $f (pid=$pid)"
    rm -f "$f"
  else
    echo "keeping live $f pid=$pid"
  fi
done
# nuclear project binary sweep (not chrome)
for pat in 'system/avatar_window' 'system/egg_window' 'system/chtpm_parser_pal' \
           'system/keyboard_input' 'system/prisc\+x' 'system/gl_mirror' \
           'system/orchestrator' 'system/renderer'; do
  # careful: only match our binaries, not chrome --type=renderer
  pids=$(pgrep -f "$pat" 2>/dev/null || true)
  if [ -n "$pids" ]; then
    echo "killing $pat: $pids"
    echo "$pids" | xargs -r kill -TERM 2>/dev/null || true
    sleep 0.15
    echo "$pids" | xargs -r kill -9 2>/dev/null || true
  fi
done

## Assistant

## Cleanup done

**Right now:** no project binaries running (`avatar_window`, `egg_window`, `renderer`, `chtpm`, `prisc+x`, etc.), and **0** stale `window.pid` files.

## Safer kill-on-quit

### `01.avatar-creation` quit path
On **Ctrl+C / exit / `button.sh kill`**:

1. Write `quit_flag.txt` (soft stop for any poll loops)
2. **SIGTERM** UI trio + desktop `avatar_window`s  
3. Short grace (~0.3s) so ~60fps loops can exit  
4. **SIGKILL** anything still alive  
5. Clear `window.pid` / `avatar_window_pids.txt`  
6. Remove session dir  

### Tracking
`open_avatar_window` now:
- Replaces any existing window for that clone  
- Writes `window.pid`  
- Appends to `pieces/system/avatar_window_pids.txt`  
- `setsid()` so windows don’t die with a weird tty, but **still get reaped on quit**

### Commands
```bash
cd 0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️
./button.sh kill          # full cleanup
./button.sh kill-windows  # only desktop avatars
```

Workspace nuclear option (safer now — matches by **`/proc/pid/exe` basename**, not cmdline, so no Chrome / no self-kill):

```bash
./EMERGENCY_KILL.sh
```

### Why “60fps kill”
Desktop windows and `chtpm_parser` poll around 16ms. If quit only killed the terminal and left windows/UI, they kept burning CPU. Quit now always reaps those first.

That background task was an earlier path-glitch run; the real cleanup already finished cleanly.

**Current status**
- No project binaries left (`avatar_window`, `egg_window`, `renderer`, `chtpm`, `prisc+x`, …)
- Stale `window.pid` files cleared
- Quit path hardened: `quit_flag` → TERM → grace → KILL, including desktop windows

```bash
cd 0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️
./button.sh kill          # full cleanup
./button.sh kill-windows  # avatars only
# or from workspace root:
./EMERGENCY_KILL.sh
```

Implementing `xyzfs/session.pdl` for login/guest identity, wiring it into character creation, then running a harness that creates a character and opens a GL window.

## Tools

- Execute: # patch login/logout with session.pdl writers via python for reliability
python3 << 'PY'
from pathlib import Path

SESSION_WRITE_FN = r'''
/* Write xyzfs/session.pdl - who is logged in (or guest). Character
 * creation and sibling apps read this as the durable session identity. */
static void write_session_pdl(const char *install,
                              const char *mode,
                              const char *user_id,
                              const char *user_uuid,
                              const char *display_name,
                              const char *xyzfs_rel,
                              const char *active_avatar_uuid,
                              const char *active_avatar_path)
{
    char dir[PATH_BUF], path[PATH_BUF];
    snprintf(dir, sizeof(dir), "%s/xyzfs", install);
    mkdir(dir, 0755);
    snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "%s/session.pdl", dir);
    FILE *f = fopen(path, "w");
    if (!f) return;
    fprintf(f, "SECTION      | KEY                | VALUE\n");
    fprintf(f, "----------------------------------------\n");
    fprintf(f, "META         | piece_id           | xyzfs_session\n");
    fprintf(f, "META         | version            | 1.0\n");
    fprintf(f, "META         | notes              | Current logged-in user or guest for this machine\n\n");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | mode                 | %s\n", mode && mode[0] ? mode : "guest");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | user_id              | %s\n", user_id ? user_id : "");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | user_uuid            | %s\n", user_uuid ? user_uuid : "");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | display_name         | %s\n", display_name && display_name[0] ? display_name : (strcmp(mode, "guest") == 0 ? "Guest" : ""));
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | xyzfs_path           | %s\n", xyzfs_rel ? xyzfs_rel : "");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | logged_in_at         | %ld\n", (long)time(NULL));
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | active_avatar_uuid   | %s\n", active_avatar_uuid ? active_avatar_uuid : "");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | active_avatar_path   | %s\n", active_avatar_path ? active_avatar_path : "");
    fprintf(f, "\n");
    fprintf(f, "# mode=logged_in | guest\n");
    fprintf(f, "# xyzfs_path is relative to 00.login-signup install root\n");
    fprintf(f, "# active_avatar_* set by 01.avatar-creation when a clone is minted/opened\n");
    fclose(f);
}

static void read_session_state(const char *install, const char *key, char *out, size_t out_sz)
{
    out[0] = '\\0';
    char path[PATH_BUF];
    snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "%s/xyzfs/session.pdl", install);
    FILE *f = fopen(path, "r");
    if (!f) return;
    char line[MAX_LINE];
    size_t kl = strlen(key);
    while (fgets(line, sizeof(line), f)) {
        if (strncmp(line, "STATE", 5) != 0) continue;
        char *p1 = strchr(line, '|');
        if (!p1) continue;
        char *k = p1 + 1;
        while (*k == ' ' || *k == '\\t') k++;
        if (strncmp(k, key, kl) != 0) continue;
        char *after = k + kl;
        while (*after == ' ' || *after == '\\t') after++;
        if (*after != '|') continue;
        char *v = after + 1;
        while (*v == ' ' || *v == '\\t') v++;
        v[strcspn(v, "\\r\\n")] = '\\0';
        /* trim trailing spaces */
        size_t n = strlen(v);
        while (n > 0 && (v[n-1] == ' ' || v[n-1] == '\\t')) v[--n] = '\\0';
        snprintf(out, out_sz, "%s", v);
        break;
    }
    fclose(f);
}
'''

# Fix the escape mess - write clean C
SESSION_WRITE_FN = r'''
/* Write xyzfs/session.pdl - who is logged in (or guest). Character
 * creation and sibling apps read this as the durable session identity. */
static void write_session_pdl(const char *install,
                              const char *mode,
                              const char *user_id,
                              const char *user_uuid,
                              const char *display_name,
                              const char *xyzfs_rel,
                              const char *active_avatar_uuid,
                              const char *active_avatar_path)
{
    char dir[PATH_BUF], path[PATH_BUF];
    snprintf(dir, sizeof(dir), "%s/xyzfs", install);
    mkdir(dir, 0755);
    snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "%s/session.pdl", dir);
    FILE *f = fopen(path, "w");
    if (!f) return;
    fprintf(f, "SECTION      | KEY                | VALUE\n");
    fprintf(f, "----------------------------------------\n");
    fprintf(f, "META         | piece_id           | xyzfs_session\n");
    fprintf(f, "META         | version            | 1.0\n");
    fprintf(f, "META         | notes              | Current logged-in user or guest for this machine\n\n");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | mode                 | %s\n", mode && mode[0] ? mode : "guest");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | user_id              | %s\n", user_id ? user_id : "");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | user_uuid            | %s\n", user_uuid ? user_uuid : "");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | display_name         | %s\n",
            (display_name && display_name[0]) ? display_name
            : ((mode && strcmp(mode, "guest") == 0) ? "Guest" : ""));
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | xyzfs_path           | %s\n", xyzfs_rel ? xyzfs_rel : "");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | logged_in_at         | %ld\n", (long)time(NULL));
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | active_avatar_uuid   | %s\n", active_avatar_uuid ? active_avatar_uuid : "");
    fprintf(f, "STATE        | active_avatar_path   | %s\n", active_avatar_path ? active_avatar_path : "");
    fprintf(f, "\n");
    fprintf(f, "# mode=logged_in | guest\n");
    fprintf(f, "# xyzfs_path is relative to 00.login-signup install root\n");
    fprintf(f, "# active_avatar_* set by 01.avatar-creation when a clone is minted/opened\n");
    fclose(f);
}

static void read_session_state(const char *install, const char *key, char *out, size_t out_sz)
{
    out[0] = '\0';
    char path[PATH_BUF];
    snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "%s/xyzfs/session.pdl", install);
    FILE *f = fopen(path, "r");
    if (!f) return;
    char line[MAX_LINE];
    size_t kl = strlen(key);
    while (fgets(line, sizeof(line), f)) {
        if (strncmp(line, "STATE", 5) != 0) continue;
        char *p1 = strchr(line, '|');
        if (!p1) continue;
        char *k = p1 + 1;
        while (*k == ' ' || *k == '\t') k++;
        if (strncmp(k, key, kl) != 0) continue;
        char *after = k + kl;
        while (*after == ' ' || *after == '\t') after++;
        if (*after != '|') continue;
        char *v = after + 1;
        while (*v == ' ' || *v == '\t') v++;
        v[strcspn(v, "\r\n")] = '\0';
        size_t n = strlen(v);
        while (n > 0 && (v[n - 1] == ' ' || v[n - 1] == '\t')) v[--n] = '\0';
        snprintf(out, out_sz, "%s", v);
        break;
    }
    fclose(f);
}
'''

login = Path("0.user-pal👤️/00.login-signup/ops/userpal_login.c")
text = login.read_text()
if "write_session_pdl" not in text:
    # insert after includes / before main - after resolve helpers is fine: before mint_uuid or after write_profile
    anchor = "static int mint_uuid"
    if anchor not in text:
        anchor = "int main(int argc"
        text = text.replace(anchor, SESSION_WRITE_FN + "\n" + anchor, 1)
    else:
        text = text.replace(anchor, SESSION_WRITE_FN + "\n" + anchor, 1)

# after writing current_login, also write session.pdl preserving active avatar if same user
old = '''    fprintf(f, "current_user_id=%s\\n", user_id);
    fprintf(f, "current_user_uuid=%s\\n", uuid);
    fprintf(f, "current_xyzfs=%s\\n", xyzfs_rel);
    fprintf(f, "logged_in_at=%ld\\n", (long)time(NULL));
    fclose(f);

    printf("Logged in as '%s'.\\n", user_id);
    return 0;
}'''
new = '''    fprintf(f, "current_user_id=%s\\n", user_id);
    fprintf(f, "current_user_uuid=%s\\n", uuid);
    fprintf(f, "current_xyzfs=%s\\n", xyzfs_rel);
    fprintf(f, "logged_in_at=%ld\\n", (long)time(NULL));
    fclose(f);

    /* Preserve active avatar if re-logging same user; else clear. */
    char prev_uuid[UUID_LEN], av_uuid[128], av_path[PATH_BUF];
    read_session_state(install_root, "user_uuid", prev_uuid, sizeof(prev_uuid));
    av_uuid[0] = av_path[0] = '\\0';
    if (prev_uuid[0] && strcmp(prev_uuid, uuid) == 0) {
        read_session_state(install_root, "active_avatar_uuid", av_uuid, sizeof(av_uuid));
        read_session_state(install_root, "active_avatar_path", av_path, sizeof(av_path));
    }
    write_session_pdl(install_root, "logged_in", user_id, uuid,
                      display_name, xyzfs_rel, av_uuid, av_path);

    printf("Logged in as '%s'.\\n", user_id);
    return 0;
}'''
if "write_session_pdl(install_root, \"logged_in\"" not in text:

login.write_text(text)
print("login patched", "write_session_pdl" in text)

# logout
logout = Path("0.user-pal👤️/00.login-signup/ops/userpal_logout.c")
lt = logout.read_text()
if "write_session_pdl" not in lt:
    # need time.h and MAX_LINE for helper
    if "#include <time.h>" not in lt:

    # inject helper before main
    lt = lt.replace("int main(void) {", SESSION_WRITE_FN + "\nint main(void) {")
    old_lo = '''    fprintf(f, "current_user_id=\\n");
    fprintf(f, "current_user_uuid=\\n");
    fprintf(f, "current_xyzfs=\\n");

## Done

### `xyzfs/session.pdl` — who is logged in
Lives at:

`0.user-pal👤️/00.login-signup/xyzfs/session.pdl`

| STATE | Meaning |
|--------|---------|
| `mode` | `logged_in` or `guest` |
| `user_id` / `user_uuid` / `display_name` | identity |
| `xyzfs_path` | `xyzfs/users/<uuid>` |
| `active_avatar_uuid` / `active_avatar_path` | last minted/opened character |

**Writers**
- **login** → `mode=logged_in` + user fields  
- **logout** → `mode=guest`  
- **generate_clone** (character create) → sets `active_avatar_*` from session identity  
- **open_avatar_window** → refreshes `active_avatar_*` when the GL window opens  

**Readers**
- Character creation prefers `session.pdl`, then `current_login.txt`, then guest fallback

            blank();
            line("  Rename/age above, Apply; or cycle DNA / desktop below.");
        }
    }

'''
    text = text[:start] + new + text[end:]
    p.write_text(text)
    print("compose updated")
PY

### Changes
- List `piece.pdl` is **select-only** (name + full uuid)
- New layout `avatar_manage.chtpm` for all customization / desktop actions
- Selecting a clone jumps via `layout_changed` to that manage screen

## User

while are teh kill scripts so slow

## Assistant

The slowness is almost certainly from scanning every `/proc` PID and stacked sleep/grace periods. Checking and speeding up the kill scripts.

## Tools

- Read: 0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/scripts/kill_avatar_windows.sh
- Read: 0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/pieces/os/kill_all.sh
- Read: EMERGENCY_KILL.sh

## Assistant

Root cause: full `/proc` scans with `readlink -f` on every PID, repeated many times, plus long sleeps and a workspace-wide `find`. Switching to exact-name `pgrep -x`/`pkill -x` and a single-pass fast path.

## Tools

- Edit: 0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/scripts/kill_avatar_windows.sh
- Edit: EMERGENCY_KILL.sh
- Edit: 0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/pieces/os/kill_all.sh
- Execute: chmod +x "0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/pieces/os/kill_all.sh" \
         "0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/scripts/kill_avatar_windows.sh" \
         EMERGENCY_KILL.sh
# time empty kill (nothing running) — should be near-instant
echo "=== time kill_all (idle) ==="
time bash "0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/pieces/os/kill_all.sh"
echo "=== time kill-windows ==="
time bash "0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👀�/scripts/kill_avatar_windows.sh" 2>/dev/null \
  || time bash "0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/scripts/kill_avatar_windows.sh"
echo "=== time EMERGENCY ==="
time bash ./EMERGENCY_KILL.sh
echo "=== time button.sh kill ==="
time bash "0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/button.sh" kill
 (Rewrite fast kills and benchmark idle path)

## Assistant

## Why they were slow

1. **Full `/proc` scan** — for every binary name, loop every PID and `readlink -f` (expensive)
2. **Repeated** — TERM pass ×5, KILL pass ×5, residual pass ×5 ≈ **15 full system scans**
3. **Long sleeps** — 0.3s + 0.1s even when nothing was running
4. **`find` over the whole workspace** in `EMERGENCY_KILL` (huge tree)

## Fix

- Use **`pgrep -x` / `pkill -x`** (exact process **name** — safe, Chrome is `chrome` not `renderer`)
- Kill from **`window.pid` / pid lists first**
- **Skip sleep** if nothing was alive
- **No tree-wide `find`**

## Idle timings now

| Script | Before (ballpark) | After |
|--------|-------------------|--------|
| `kill_all.sh` | multi-second | **~0.17s** |
| `kill_avatar_windows.sh` | ~0.5s+ | **~0.04s** |
| `button.sh kill` | multi-second | **~0.24s** |
| `EMERGENCY_KILL.sh` | multi-second | **~0.4s** |

## Assistant

Yes — investigated and wrote it up in **`+-picker.txt`** at the workspace root.

### Two mechanisms (easy to mix)

| Piece | Role | Where |
|--------|------|--------|
| **`<scroller>`** | Horizontal strip with `[current]` | op-ed **MAPS** / **GLYPHS** |
| **`[+]` / `[-]` fold** | Collapse children until Enter opens them | parser-native; **`+-demo`** is the tree example |

op-ed map select is **scroller only** (always on the GUI). For skin tone you want **both**: folded by default, strip only when opened.

### How map select works

```xml
<scroller id="MAPS" label="${map_palette_view}" onClick="INTERACT" />
```

- Manager builds `map_palette_view` like `ADD [map_0001.txt] map_0002.txt`
- Focus slot **2** → `a`/`d` move `map_idx`, **Enter** commits → `stage_map_idx`
- Parser only substitutes the label; it does not know maps

### How fold works

- Label has `[+]` (default closed) or `[-]` (default open)
- State in `gui_state` as `fold_<id>` = `open` | `folded`
- Enter toggles + re-parse; children of folded parents are not drawn/navigable

### Skin-tone target (in the doc)

```
[+] Skin tone  👨🏽          ← closed, one line
[-] Skin tone  👨🏽
[>] SKIN: 👨 👨🏻 [👨🏼] 👨🏽 …  ← open strip, a/d + Enter commit
```

### Good news

Our **`chtpm_parser_pal.c`** already has scroller + fold. No parser port needed. Remaining work is app-side (vars, keys, layout replace of “Cycle Skin Tone”).

Read `+-picker.txt` and say if that matches what you want; next step is implementing the skin fold+scroller.

## Assistant

Implemented. Fold+scroller DNA pickers are live on manage/customize.

### What you get

**Manage screen** (`avatar_manage.chtpm`):
- `[+] Skin`, `[+] Gender`, `[+] Hair`, … — collapsed by default
- Enter on a fold → opens strip
- Enter on the strip → INTERACT mode
- **`a` / `d` (or arrows)** → move `[brackets]` and **apply DNA live** (local + xyzfs)

**Methods left** (no more Cycle* rows):
- Apply Name / Age  
- Open Desktop Window  
- Sleep / Wake  

### How it works

| Layer | Role |
|--------|------|
| Layout | `[+]` folds + `<scroller>` strips |
| `avatar_compose_frame` | Builds `skin_palette_view=👨 [👨🏻] 👨🏼 …` into `gui_state` |
| `avatar_menu_input` | On `a`/`d` under INTERACT → `cycle_dna set index` |
| `cycle_dna` | Now supports `cycle_dna.+x <uuid> <field> <index>` |

### Design note (Enter)

Enter is **not** used to commit DNA. Rename/age `cli_io` also injects Enter on the same relay, so that would corrupt DNA. Scrolling applies immediately (same idea as op-ed **glyph** palette).

### Verified

Harness `demo_menu_fx.sh`: **OVERALL: PASS**  
- select clone → `d` moves skin index → palette vars + brackets written → desktop window opens

### Files touched

- `pieces/chtpm/layouts/avatar_manage.chtpm`, `customize.chtpm`
- `projects/.../avatar_manage/piece.pdl`
- `ops/cycle_dna.c`, `avatar_compose_frame.c`, `avatar_menu_input.c`
- harness + `+-picker.txt` checklist updated

In the live GUI: open **Manage** on a clone → focus **`[+] Skin`** → Enter → Enter on the strip → `a`/`d` to change skin.

## Assistant

Here’s what was going wrong and what we fixed.

### Choose Clone — still there, was easy to miss
- List screen was titled **“AVATARS”** and main only said **“Avatars”**.
- **Back** from manage was only an `href`, so state/list rebuild was flaky.

**Now:**
- Main → **Choose Clone**
- List title → **CHOOSE CLONE**
- Manage has method **Back to Choose Clone** (`DESELECT`) — clears selection, rebuilds the list, opens `avatars.chtpm`

### Manage “spam without changes”
Several thrash paths:

1. **Idle loop rewrote `piece.pdl` every ~30ms** on the list screen  
2. **Compose always rewrote `view.txt` + pinged `frame_changed`** even when nothing changed  
3. **DNA `gui_state` rewritten every compose** (even identical values)  
4. **`a`/`d` could apply DNA with no fold open** (defaulted to skin)

**Now:**
- Rebuild list only when **entering** Choose Clone  
- Compose **only pings when panel text actually changes**  
- Palette keys only written if value changed  
- `a`/`d` DNA only if a DNA fold is **`fold_*=open`**

### Flow again
```
Main → Choose Clone → (pick row) → Manage Clone
         ↑                              │
         └──── Back to Choose Clone ────┘
```

Harness: **OVERALL: PASS** (including back-to-choose-clone + no-op compose).

Restart with `./button.sh run` so you get a clean session. From main, open **Choose Clone**; from manage use **Back to Choose Clone**.

## Assistant

### Why it skipped Choose Clone

Not auto-magic from “only one clone” — **stale key replay**.

1. Store → Claim Free injects `KEY:2` into `interact_relay.txt`
2. You open **Choose Clone** (`href` restarts the module; read cursor resets to 0)
3. Loop re-reads old `KEY:2`
4. On Choose Clone, `KEY:2` = **first METHOD** = `SELECT_AVATAR:…` → jumps to Manage

So it looked like “click went past the list.”

### Fixes

| Change | Effect |
|--------|--------|
| **Clear `interact_relay` on every screen change** | No leftover Store/Faucet keys |
| **Ignore non-zero key on the same tick as a screen change** | Replay can’t SELECT |
| **Clear relay on `request_layout`** | Clean handoff to Manage / back |
| **Removed `${game_map}` from main** | Stops double panel box |

Choose Clone with **0 or 1** clones now stays put until you **Enter a clone row**.

Harness: **OVERALL: PASS** including `no auto-select on enter choose-clone`.

Restart with `./button.sh run` and try again: Main → Choose Clone should list (or empty) and wait.

## Assistant

Explaining findings first, then applying the minimal fixes.

### 1) Skin fold: why `[^]` / selector “disappears” and you can’t scroll

Layout is:
```xml
<button id="skin_pick" label="[+] Skin …" onClick="ACTIVATE">
  <scroller id="SKIN" … onClick="INTERACT" />
</button>
```

What the parser actually does:

1. **Enter on `[+] Skin`**  
   - Toggles fold open (`[-]`) and re-parses.  
   - Then runs **ACTIVATE**: parent becomes `active_index`, focus moves to the SKIN scroller.  
   - While ACTIVATE is active, render **strips `[+]`/`[-]` from the label** (you see `[^] 7. [ Skin …]` without `+`/`-`). That’s why it looked like the markers were “removed.”

2. **Enter again on the SKIN strip (the bug)**  
   With the parent still ACTIVATE-active, Enter is handled in the **submenu** branch, not the normal INTERACT path.  
   For the child it effectively does `send_command("INTERACT")`.  
   **`INTERACT` is not a real `send_command` verb** — it does *not* set `active_index` to the scroller.  
   So the strip never gets true INTERACT (`[^]` + a/d). Focus/selector looks broken; a/d never reach the DNA picker.

**What we will do (no redesign of folds):**
- In the ACTIVATE submenu Enter path: if focused child has `onClick="INTERACT"`, **activate that child** the same way nav-mode does (`active_index = focus`, show `[^]`, a/d work).  
- **Stop stripping `[+]`/`[-]`** when an ACTIVATE parent is active so fold state stays visible.  
- Keep `[+]` / `[-]` folds and the scroller as they are — only fix activation.

### 2) Faucet money / clones not on the user account

Live state:
- `current_login.txt` is **empty**  
- `xyzfs/session.pdl` has the real login (`mode=logged_in`, `xyzfs_path=xyzfs/users/…`)

`claim_tokens` (and list inventory) only read **`current_login.txt`**.  
`generate_clone` already prefers **`session.pdl`**.

So faucet often writes **local `user_01`**, not `xyzfs/users/<uuid>/home/wallet.txt`. Same mismatch for inventory load → clones look “forgotten.”

**What we will do:**  
Make faucet (and shared identity/inventory lookup) use the same session resolution as `generate_clone`: **session.pdl first, then current_login, then guest local.**

Implementing those two fixes now.

## Assistant

### What was wrong (researched, not guessed)

**Skin / DNA scroller**

1. Enter on `[+] Skin` correctly opens the fold and runs **ACTIVATE** (parent gets `[^]`).
2. Enter again on the **SKIN** strip was handled inside the ACTIVATE submenu as `send_command("INTERACT")`.
3. **`INTERACT` is not a command verb** — so the scroller never became `active_index`.
4. Result: strip never stuck as `[^]`, a/d never scrolled; it felt like the selector vanished.
5. Separately, ACTIVATE render was **stripping `[+]`/`[-]`** from the label — that was intentional old code, not something we should “redesign away.”

**Faucet / clones on “user account”**

- Real login lives in `xyzfs/session.pdl` (`mode=logged_in`).
- `current_login.txt` is often **empty**.
- Faucet/list only read `current_login` → money went to **local guest**, list missed **xyzfs inventory**.

### What we changed (minimal)

| Change | Why |
|--------|-----|
| Parser: Enter on child with `onClick="INTERACT"` under ACTIVATE → set `active_index` to that child | Sub-strip can truly activate (`[^]`) so a/d works |
| Parser: **stop stripping** `[+]`/`[-]` when ACTIVATE is on | Keep fold markers visible |
| Faucet / buy / compose / choose-clone list: identity from **session.pdl first** | Tokens + clones hit the real user wallet/inventory |

**Not removed:** fold `[+]`/`[-]`, scroller, ACTIVATE on the skin parent.

### How to use skin now

1. Enter on `[+] Skin` → opens `[-] Skin` + strip  
2. Enter on `SKIN: 👨 [👨🏻] …` → strip shows `[^]`  
3. `a` / `d` (or arrows) cycle and apply  
4. Esc leaves INTERACT  

### Verified

- Faucet with logged-in session: `Claimed 10 tokens! Balance: 10 (user wallet)`  
- Choose Clone methods load from `xyzfs/users/…/home/avatars/inventory.txt`

Restart with `./button.sh run` so the new parser binary is live.

## Assistant

### Why it was stuck

With **ACTIVATE + INTERACT**, the strip showed `[^]` and **all keys** (digits, w/s) were swallowed as INTERACT. You couldn’t jump to row 10 / Hair — only a/d went to the module, and nav felt broken.

### What we changed

1. **Removed `ACTIVATE` from DNA folds** — only `[+]` / `[-]` fold remains.  
   When a fold is **open**, the strip is a **normal navigable row with its own number** (e.g. `8. SKIN`, `9. GENDER`), same as Apply / Sleep.

2. **a/d work when the strip is focused** (`[>]`) — no need to trap in `[^]`.  
   Number-jump to that row, then a/d.

3. **If you still Enter into INTERACT (`[^`])**, w/s / digits / Esc **leave INTERACT** and navigate normally again. Only a/d stay on the strip.

### How to use it now

```
7. [+] Skin
   → Enter opens
8. SKIN : 👨 [👨🏻] 👨🏼 …
   → press 8 (or w/s to it), then a/d to change
9. [+] Gender

python3 << 'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path("#.haiku+/!.pal-pitfalls+1.txt")
text = p.read_text()
marker = "=======================================================================\nEND OF PAL-PITFALLS"
pit = '''
=======================================================================
PITFALL: DNA / palette pickers MUST be numbered nav rows — not a/d scrollers
=======================================================================
DATE: 2026-07-27
PROJECT: 0.user-pal/01.avatar-creation

WRONG: a single <scroller> strip like `SKIN: 👨 [👨🏻] 👨🏼 …` with a/d (or
INTERACT lock) to cycle. User tries to "select" a skin and either
navigates away (w/s/digits swallowed or strip steals focus) or has no
per-option index to jump to. Deviated from std chtpm menu pattern.

RIGHT (std): under a [+]/[-] fold, inject one <button> per option so
the parser assigns real navigable indexes:

  [ ] 7. [+] Skin  👨🏻
  (Enter open)
  [ ] 7. [-] Skin  👨🏻
  [>] 8. [👨🏻]          ← KEY:301 applies skin index 1
  [ ] 9. 👨🏼
  …

Implementation notes for avatar-creation:
  - compose writes dna_opts_skin=… markup into gui_state (lazy XML sub)
  - each button onClick="KEY:300+idx" (gender 310+, hair 320+, …)
  - avatar_menu_input maps those key codes → cycle_dna.+x <uuid> <field> <idx>
  - fold [+]/[-] only hides/shows children; no ACTIVATE trap, no a/d strip

ALSO: do not put onClick="ACTIVATE" on DNA folds if children need global
numbered nav — ACTIVATE scopes navigability and INTERACT-on-child was
broken via send_command("INTERACT") (not a real command verb).

FILES: ops/avatar_compose_frame.c, ops/avatar_menu_input.c,
       pieces/chtpm/layouts/avatar_manage.chtpm, customize.chtpm

'''
if "DNA / palette pickers MUST be numbered" not in text:
    if marker in text:
        text = text.replace(marker, pit + marker)
    else:
        text = text.rstrip() + "\n" + pit
    p.write_text(text)
    print("pitfall appended")
else:
    print("pitfall already present")
PY

## Assistant

You’re right — I had the wrong pattern. **Each skin (and DNA choice) is a numbered menu row**, not one a/d strip. That *is* std chtpm. Documented in `#.haiku+/!.pal-pitfalls+1.txt`, and **harness-tested** (`OVERALL: PASS`).

### What you get now

```
7. [+] Skin  👨🏻          ← Enter opens fold
7. [-] Skin  👨🏻
8. 👨                    ← own index, arrows/digits work
9. [👨🏻]                  ← current (brackets)
10. 👨🏼
11. 👨🏽                   ← Enter applies that tone
…
```

Same for Gender / Hair / Shirt / Pants / Height / Weight.

### How it works

| Piece | Role |
|--------|------|
| Fold `[+]` / `[-]` | Show/hide options only |
| One `<button>` per option | Real nav numbers |
| `KEY:300+idx` etc. | Apply via `cycle_dna` |
| Current choice | Label like `[👨🏽]` |

No scroller, no a/d trap, no ACTIVATE lock.

### Tested

- Compose writes `dna_opts_skin=…KEY:300…`
- `KEY:303` → skin index 3, brackets update  
- Full `demo_menu_fx.sh`: **PASS**

Restart with `./button.sh run`, open **[-] Skin**, then use **digits / arrows** on each tone and **Enter** to apply.

## Assistant

### What mutaclysm does (investigated)

| Piece | Role |
|--------|------|
| `gl_mirror` | GLUT RGB window — blits frame texture |
| `compose_rgb_frame` | 3D software view |
| `camera_control` | **q/e** yaw, **r/t** pitch, **w/s** pan/zoom, **f** reset, modes **1–3** |

### What we added

**RGB character preview** (manager view), separate from the **2D desktop pet**.

| | **RGB Preview** (new) | **Desktop Window** (existing) |
|--|------------------------|-------------------------------|
| Binary | `system/character_preview` | `system/avatar_window` |
| When | Auto on **Choose Clone → manage** | Method **Open Desktop Window (2D pet)** |
| Look | 3D orbit + ground grid + sprite billboard | Shaped 2D pet on desktop |
| Cam | muta-style: **q/e r/t w/s f 1/2/3**, mouse drag | — |

### Controls (focus the RGB window)

- **q / e** — yaw  
- **r / t** — pitch  
- **w / s** — zoom  
- **f** — reset  
- **1 / 2 / 3** — third / portrait / free presets  
- **drag** — orbit  
- Live reloads sprite when DNA/`state.txt` changes  

### Menu

- **Reopen RGB Preview** if you closed it  
- **Back to Choose Clone** kills the preview  

### Harness

`demo_menu_fx.sh`: **OVERALL: PASS** (desktop + RGB preview both spawn).

Restart with `./button.sh run`, pick a clone — RGB preview should open with the character; use **Open Desktop Window** for the 2D pet.

# Read color helpers from hair/shirt - simple RGB maps
python3 << 'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path("0.user-pal👤️/01.avatar-creation👤️/system/character_preview.c")
text = p.read_text()
# Insert color parse helpers before display
helper = r'''
/* --- Minecraft-ish blocky avatar (box head/torso/arms/legs) --- */
static void hex_approx(const char *name, float *r, float *g, float *b) {
    /* rough palette for DNA color names + skin emoji index fallbacks */
    *r = 0.7f; *g = 0.55f; *b = 0.4f;
    if (!name || !name[0]) return;
    if (strstr(name, "black")) { *r=0.15f;*g=0.12f;*b=0.1f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "brown")) { *r=0.45f;*g=0.28f;*b=0.12f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "blonde") || strstr(name, "yellow")) { *r=0.9f;*g=0.8f;*b=0.35f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "red")) { *r=0.75f;*g=0.2f;*b=0.15f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "gray") || strstr(name, "grey")) { *r=0.55f;*g=0.55f;*b=0.55f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "blue")) { *r=0.25f;*g=0.4f;*b=0.85f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "pink")) { *r=0.9f;*g=0.5f;*b=0.65f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "green")) { *r=0.25f;*g=0.65f;*b=0.3f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "white")) { *r=0.92f;*g=0.92f;*b=0.95f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "purple")) { *r=0.55f;*g=0.3f;*b=0.75f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "orange")) { *r=0.9f;*g=0.5f;*b=0.15f; }
    else if (strstr(name, "khaki")) { *r=0.7f;*g=0.65f;*b=0.4f; }
}

static void skin_tone_rgb(int idx, float *r, float *g, float *b) {
    /* approximate Unicode Fitzpatrick-ish tones */
    switch (idx) {
    case 0: *r=0.96f; *g=0.80f; *b=0.69f; break; /* default */
    case 1: *r=0.98f; *g=0.87f; *b=0.76f; break; /* light */
    case 2: *r=0.90f; *g=0.72f; *b=0.58f; break;
    case 3: *r=0.76f; *g=0.55f; *b=0.40f; break;
    case 4: *r=0.55f; *g=0.36f; *b=0.25f; break;
    case 5: *r=0.35f; *g=0.22f; *b=0.15f; break;
    default: *r=0.85f; *g=0.65f; *b=0.5f; break;
    }
}

static void draw_box(float x0, float y0, float z0, float x1, float y1, float z1,
                     float r, float g, float b) {
    glColor3f(r, g, b);
    /* 6 faces */
    glBegin(GL_QUADS);
    /* +Z */
    glVertex3f(x0,y0,z1); glVertex3f(x1,y0,z1); glVertex3f(x1,y1,z1); glVertex3f(x0,y1,z1);
    /* -Z darker */
    glColor3f(r*0.75f,g*0.75f,b*0.75f);
    glVertex3f(x1,y0,z0); glVertex3f(x0,y0,z0); glVertex3f(x0,y1,z0); glVertex3f(x1,y1,z0);
    /* +X */
    glColor3f(r*0.9f,g*0.9f,b*0.9f);
    glVertex3f(x1,y0,z1); glVertex3f(x1,y0,z0); glVertex3f(x1,y1,z0); glVertex3f(x1,y1,z1);
    /* -X */
    glColor3f(r*0.85f,g*0.85f,b*0.85f);
    glVertex3f(x0,y0,z0); glVertex3f(x0,y0,z1); glVertex3f(x0,y1,z1); glVertex3f(x0,y1,z0);
    /* +Y top */
    glColor3f(r*1.05f > 1?1:r*1.05f, g*1.05f>1?1:g*1.05f, b*1.05f>1?1:b*1.05f);
    glVertex3f(x0,y1,z1); glVertex3f(x1,y1,z1); glVertex3f(x1,y1,z0); glVertex3f(x0,y1,z0);
    /* -Y */
    glColor3f(r*0.7f,g*0.7f,b*0.7f);
    glVertex3f(x0,y0,z0); glVertex3f(x1,y0,z0); glVertex3f(x1,y0,z1); glVertex3f(x0,y0,z1);
    glEnd();
}

static void draw_mc_character(void) {
    char si[16]="", hair[32]="", shirt[32]="", pants[32]="";
    read_kv(state_path, "skin_index", si, sizeof(si));
    read_kv(state_path, "hair_color", hair, sizeof(hair));
    read_kv(state_path, "shirt_color", shirt, sizeof(shirt));
    read_kv(state_path, "pants_color", pants, sizeof(pants));
    float sr,sg,sb, hr,hg,hb, tr,tg,tb, pr,pg,pb;
    skin_tone_rgb(atoi(si), &sr,&sg,&sb);
    hex_approx(hair, &hr,&hg,&hb);
    hex_approx(shirt, &tr,&tg,&tb);
    hex_approx(pants, &pr,&pg,&pb);

    glDisable(GL_TEXTURE_2D);
    glPushMatrix();
    /* units roughly like MC: head 0.5 cube, body 0.5x0.75x0.25, etc. */
    /* legs */
    draw_box(-0.22f, 0.0f, -0.10f, -0.02f, 0.55f, 0.10f, pr,pg,pb);
    draw_box( 0.02f, 0.0f, -0.10f,  0.22f, 0.55f, 0.10f, pr*0.92f,pg*0.92f,pb*0.92f);
    /* torso */
    draw_box(-0.25f, 0.55f, -0.12f, 0.25f, 1.15f, 0.12f, tr,tg,tb);
    /* arms */
    draw_box(-0.42f, 0.55f, -0.10f, -0.25f, 1.15f, 0.10f, sr,sg,sb);
    draw_box( 0.25f, 0.55f, -0.10f,  0.42f, 1.15f, 0.10f, sr*0.95f,sg*0.95f,sb*0.95f);
    /* sleeves tint on upper arm strip */
    draw_box(-0.42f, 0.95f, -0.105f, -0.25f, 1.15f, 0.105f, tr*0.9f,tg*0.9f,tb*0.9f);
    draw_box( 0.25f, 0.95f, -0.105f,  0.42f, 1.15f, 0.105f, tr*0.9f,tg*0.9f,tb*0.9f);
    /* head */
    draw_box(-0.22f, 1.15f, -0.22f, 0.22f, 1.59f, 0.22f, sr,sg,sb);
    /* hair top + bangs */
    draw_box(-0.24f, 1.48f, -0.24f, 0.24f, 1.62f, 0.24f, hr,hg,hb);
    draw_box(-0.24f, 1.40f, 0.18f, 0.24f, 1.55f, 0.26f, hr,hg,hb);
    /* simple face eyes */
    glColor3f(0.05f,0.05f,0.08f);
    glBegin(GL_QUADS);
    glVertex3f(-0.12f,1.38f,0.221f); glVertex3f(-0.04f,1.38f,0.221f);
    glVertex3f(-0.04f,1.44f,0.221f); glVertex3f(-0.12f,1.44f,0.221f);
    glVertex3f( 0.04f,1.38f,0.221f); glVertex3f( 0.12f,1.38f,0.221f);
    glVertex3f( 0.12f,1.44f,0.221f); glVertex3f( 0.04f,1.44f,0.221f);
    glEnd();
    glPopMatrix();
}

'''
# insert before static void display
if "draw_mc_character" not in text:
    text = text.replace("static void display(void) {", helper + "\nstatic void display(void) {")

old_char = '''    /* Character billboard: upright quad facing camera (yaw only). */
    glPushMatrix();
    glTranslatef(0.0f, 0.95f, 0.0f);
    glRotatef((float)cam_yaw, 0.0f, 1.0f, 0.0f); /* face camera orbit */
    if (g_has_tex) {
        glEnable(GL_TEXTURE_2D);
        glBindTexture(GL_TEXTURE_2D, g_tex);
        glColor3f(1, 1, 1);
        glBegin(GL_QUADS);
        glTexCoord2f(0, 1); glVertex3f(-0.7f, -0.9f, 0);
        glTexCoord2f(1, 1); glVertex3f( 0.7f, -0.9f, 0);
        glTexCoord2f(1, 0); glVertex3f( 0.7f,  0.9f, 0);
        glTexCoord2f(0, 0); glVertex3f(-0.7f,  0.9f, 0);
        glEnd();
        glDisable(GL_TEXTURE_2D);
    } else {
        /* Fallback mannequin if no sprite yet */
        glColor3f(0.55f, 0.72f, 0.95f);
        glBegin(GL_QUADS);
        glVertex3f(-0.45f, -0.9f, 0);
        glVertex3f( 0.45f, -0.9f, 0);
        glVertex3f( 0.45f,  0.5f, 0);
        glVertex3f(-0.45f,  0.5f, 0);
        glEnd();
        glColor3f(0.95f, 0.85f, 0.70f);
        glBegin(GL_TRIANGLE_FAN);
        glVertex3f(0, 0.75f, 0);
        for (int i = 0; i <= 24; i++) {
            float a = (float)i * 2.0f * (float)M_PI / 24.0f;
            glVertex3f(0.28f * cosf(a), 0.75f + 0.28f * sinf(a), 0);
        }
        glEnd();
    }
    glPopMatrix();
'''
new_char = '''    /* Minecraft-style blocky character (DNA colors from state.txt). */
    draw_mc_character();
'''
if old_char in text:
    text = text.replace(old_char, new_char)
    p.write_text(text)
    print("preview updated to MC blocky")
else:
    print("pattern not found")
    # show snippet around Character
    i = text.find("Character")
    print(repr(text[i:i+200]) if i>=0 else "no Character")
PY

## Assistant

### Why you “had a user” but no saves

UI read **session.pdl** (`afx_1962682`) but:

1. That user’s **xyzfs dir did not exist**
2. DNA mods only wrote **local lobby** (`current_login.txt` was empty)
3. Opening windows **rewrote session** from empty login and could wipe identity
4. Guests never got a durable `guest-<uuid>` store

So relaunch looked like “forgot” the clone.

### Fixes

| Fix | Behavior |
|-----|----------|
| **`ensure_user_identity.+x`** | Logged-in → create `xyzfs/users/<uuid>/…`. Guest → **`guest-<uuid>`** + `xyzfs/users/guest-<uuid>/` |
| **`hydrate_avatars.+x`** | Pull inventory → lobby; **push** local clones missing on xyzfs |
| **`button.sh run`** | Runs both on every start |
| **`cycle_dna` / name / sleep** | Sync via **session.pdl** xyzfs path (not only empty `current_login`) |
| **`generate_clone`** | Always saves under user or `guest-*` xyzfs |
| **window open** | Only updates `active_avatar_*` — **does not wipe** user |

### Verified

- Ensure → created `xyzfs/users/996bdddc-…/home/`
- Hydrate → **pushed** `ae96…` into inventory  
- DNA skin → **local + xyzfs both `skin_index=4`**
- Guest ensure → `guest-747d5f6c` → `xyzfs/users/guest-<full-uuid>/`

### Preview

RGB preview draws a **Minecraft-style blocky figure** (head/torso/arms/legs) using DNA skin/hair/shirt/pants colors + orbit cam.

Restart with `./button.sh run` — your user line should keep tokens/clones, and guests get durable `guest-<uuid>` saves.

## Assistant

### Why it was a blue circle

`Open Desktop Window` loads `system/avatar_window`. With **no `sprite.csv`**, it fell back to `draw_circle()` — the solid blue disc. Clones never got a real sprite built (`emoji_gen_atlas` is skipped), so you always hit that fallback.

### Fix

Desktop pet now draws a **front-facing Minecraft-style 2D body** from your DNA:

- head + hair (hair color)  
- torso (shirt)  
- arms (skin)  
- legs (pants)  
- simple face  

Built as a transparent RGBA sprite so the window **shape** matches the character (not a circle).

It also **rebuilds when DNA changes** (skin/hair/shirt/pants) while the window is open.

### Still separate

| | **Desktop window** | **RGB preview** |
|--|--------------------|-----------------|
| Method | Open Desktop Window (2D pet) | Auto on manage / Reopen RGB Preview |
| View | Front-facing 2D MC body | 3D orbit of same blocky figure |

Restart / recompile (`./button.sh compile` or rebuild), open manage, **Open Desktop Window** — you should see your character, not a blue circle.

## Assistant

### Short answer

**Before:** `METHOD` rows always became **buttons**. `cli_io` only existed as layout tags, so Rename/Age had to sit outside `${piece_methods}` and stole slots 1–2. That *was* a real gap — inputs should be first-class methods.

**Now:** parser supports:

```text
METHOD | Rename | CLI_IO:avatar_name_input
METHOD | Age    | CLI_IO:avatar_age_input:numeric
```

Those expand to real `<cli_io>` fields and get **normal nav numbers**.

### Manage order now

1. **Open Chara (desktop 2D)**  
2. **Rename** (type field)  
3. **Age** (numeric field)  
4. Apply Name / Age  
5. Reopen RGB Preview  
6. Sleep / Wake  
7. Back to Choose Clone  

### How to use Rename/Age

Focus **2** or **3** → **Enter** to type → **Enter**/Apply to save (same as other cli_io).

Rebuild/restart so the new parser is loaded (`./button.sh compile` then `run`).

## Assistant

### What I think you said

**1. Account switching**  
You want to know if login/signup can **switch among existing accounts** (see a list of known users, pick one, log in). If that isn’t there, you want that as the next feature.

**2. Harness config (avatar-creation app)**  
You want the test harness to be **macro-driven**: a declared list of available tests, and a simple config that says **which one(s) to run** — not always hard-wiring a single script path by hand.

---

### Current state (login)

| Have | Don’t have |
|------|------------|
| Create Account / Log In / Log Out | **No account list UI** |
| `users/<user_id>/` + `xyzfs/users/<uuid>/` on disk | No “switch to this account” picker |
| Type User ID → Log In | No browse/select from known accounts |

So: multi-account **data** exists; multi-account **chooser** does not. Switching today = type the User ID + Log In (or Log Out first).

---

### What I’d plan to do next

#### A. Account list / switch (login-signup)

- Scan `users/` (and/or session history).
- UI (same style as Choose Clone): numbered rows per account.
- Enter → `userpal_login` for that id; clear + set `current_login` + `session.pdl`.
- Guest path stays separate (`guest-<uuid>` we already use for saves).
- Optional: show “current” marker; Log Out returns to picker/login.

#### B. Harness macro config (avatar-creation)

Something like:

```text
# test-harn-same/run.conf  (or harness.pdl)
tests:
  demo_menu_fx
  demo_session_character_window
  # future: dna_persist, open_chara, etc.

run: demo_menu_fx          # or: all / list
```

- One entry script: `./test-harn-same/run.sh` reads config → runs selected scenario(s).
- Scenario scripts stay as they are under `scenarios/`.
- Easy to flip “what we actually wanna run” without editing bash paths every time.

---

### Order I’d do it

1. **Confirm this plan with you** (this message).

## Assistant

**102.editor** is scaffolded and verified — INTERACT canvas editing only, on house pal-native architecture.

### What it is
Greenfield app under `102.editor-📄️00.00/`, same shape as login-signup:

| Layer | Role |
|--------|------|
| `button.sh` + session dir | Launch (renderer → chtpm → prisc+x module) |
| `editor.chtpm` | Layout: `${game_map}` + **EDIT TEXT (INTERACT)** + methods |
| `pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal` | compose → read `interact_relay` → `menu_input` |
| `editor_menu_input` | Typing while INTERACT; NEW / CLEAR / EXIT as methods |
| `editor_compose_frame` | Chrome + buffer + `[X]` cursor → `view.txt` |

### Behavior (matches `editor-flow.txt`)
1. Focus **EDIT TEXT (INTERACT)** → Enter → `[^]` active  
2. Type keys → buffer updates, canvas shows `new[X]`  
3. Esc exits INTERACT  
4. **CLEAR FILE** empties buffer  

### Harness (all PASS)
```
00  AGY chrome + seed "hi…" + [X]
01  Enter → Active [^] INTERACT
02  type "new" → canvas new[X]
04  CLEAR → empty [X]
```
Proof: `102.editor-📄️00.00/proof/harness-20260727-215259/`

### Run it
```bash
cd 102.editor-📄️00.00
./button.sh compile
./button.sh run          # interactive terminal
# or
./test-harn-same/button.sh demo
```

**Scope held:** INTERACT mode + canvas editing only — no file browser, no full OS shell. NEW/CLEAR/EXIT are the minimal method row from the flow.

## Assistant

**Short answer:** the first harness did **not** cover that. It only typed `"new"` then CLEAR.

Now it does — ops + harness extended and **ALL PASS**.

### What was added

| Input | Keycode | Behavior |
|--------|---------|----------|
| printable | 32–126 | insert at cursor |
| backspace | 127 | delete left |
| Enter (line change) | 13 | insert `\n` |
| ← → | 1000 / 1001 | move by char |
| ↑ ↓ | 1002 / 1003 | move by **line**, same column |

### Proof frames (`proof/harness-20260727-215633/`)

| Step | Frame | Result |
|------|--------|--------|
| 02 | `abxy[X]` | type |
| 03 | `ab[X]` | **backspace** ×2 |
| 04 | `abcd` / `ef[X]` | **Enter** newline + type |
| 05 | `[X]ef` | **←** ×2 |
| 06 | `e[X]f` | **→** |
| 07 | `ab[X]cd` | **↑** line change |
| 08 | `ef[X]` | **↓** line change |
| 09 | `abZ[X]cd` | mid-line insert after ↑ |
| 11 | `[X]` | CLEAR |

```
=== ALL PASS ===
```

# also keep starred name the user asked for
out2 = p / "*.start-button-plan.md"
content = """# START BUTTON — Piecemark-style Project Loader (Plan)

**Status:** plan only — implement after approval  
**Target dir:** `*.START_BUTTON/` under house root (44.xyz)  
**TPMOS reference:** `1.TPMOS_c_+rmmp.0103.0001`  
  - layout: `pieces/apps/playrm/layouts/loader.chtpm`  
  - scanner: `pieces/apps/playrm/loader/loader_module.c`  
  - catalog PDL: `pieces/apps/playrm/loader/loader.pdl`  
  - project card: `projects/<name>/project.pdl`  
**House parity base:** `102.editor` + `0.user-pal/00.login-signup` (pal-native: button.sh, chtpm, prisc+x, compose, menu_input, harness)

---

## 1. What the TPMOS loader actually does

Frame the user pasted is **not** a hand-written button list. It is generated.

### Flow (TPMOS)

```
loader_module.+x (daemon / module under loader.chtpm)
    |
    +-- resolve project_root from pieces/locations/location_kvp
    +-- scan <project_root>/projects/*  (dirs only)
    +-- keep only dirs that contain project.pdl
    +-- sort alpha
    +-- rewrite loader.pdl:
    |      METHOD | <name> | LOAD_PROJECT:<name>
    +-- write gui_state.txt:
           project_list=<markup of <button onClick=\"LOAD_PROJECT:name\"> rows>
loader.chtpm renders:
    chrome (PIECEMARK LOADER box)
    ${project_list}   <-- dynamic buttons
    Back to Main Menu (href)
On activate:
    parser send_command(\"LOAD_PROJECT:name\")
      -> read projects/<name>/project.pdl META.entry_layout
      -> fall back to project_routes.kvp / probe layouts
      -> rewrite player_app manager state
      -> switch current_layout to entry .chtpm (same process)
```

### Key files / contracts

| Piece | Role |
|-------|------|
| `loader.chtpm` | Static chrome + `${project_list}` + Back |
| `loader.pdl` | Regenerated METHOD table (also feeds piece_methods if used) |
| `project.pdl` | Per-project card: `entry_layout`, title, start state |
| `location_kvp` | `project_root=...` (absolute) |
| `LOAD_PROJECT:` | Parser builtin: in-process layout switch |
| `loader_module.c` | Scan + rewrite PDL + write project_list markup |

### Why this is better than \"find every button.sh\"

- **Explicit membership:** only dirs with `project.pdl` appear (intentional publish, not every folder).  
- **Metadata:** entry layout / title / author live in PDL.  
- **Stable order:** alpha sort after scan.  
- **UI is data:** menu is regenerated markup, not a hardcoded chtpm list.

---

## 2. What the house needs instead (adaptation)

House apps under 44.xyz are **sibling folders**, each with its own `button.sh run` session (login, editor, mutaclsym, pal-forum, …). They are **not** all under one TPMOS `projects/` tree with shared chtpm process.

So pure `LOAD_PROJECT:` (same-process layout switch) is the wrong default launch for most house apps.

| TPMOS | House START_BUTTON |
|-------|--------------------|
| Scan `projects/` | Scan configurable **catalog root** (default: house `./`) |
| Gate: `project.pdl` | Gate: `project.pdl` **or** fallback `button.sh` (config) |
| Command: `LOAD_PROJECT:name` | Command: `RUN:relpath` (spawn `button.sh run`) primary; optional later `LOAD_PROJECT` for true in-tree chtpm projects |
| Module: loader_module daemon | Ops: `start_scan` + `start_menu_input` + `start_compose` (editor/login shape) |
| One shared OS | Launcher is its own small pal app; selected app gets **own session** |

---

## 3. Goals (v1 — nothing more, nothing less)

1. **Pal-native launcher UI** in `*.START_BUTTON/` matching the loader chrome (box + numbered project rows + nav).  
2. **Config in PDL** so catalog root / filters are not hardcoded.  
3. **Regenerate catalog PDL** by scanning (TPMOS `update_loader_pdl` parity).  
4. **Select a row → launch** that app via its `button.sh run` (or record message if missing).  
5. **Harness frames** prove: scan lists known apps, focus+enter updates message / launch marker.  
6. **No** full GL-OS, no nested OS chrome, no rewriting every house app in this PR.

---

## 4. Config PDL (change scan root later)

**File:** `*.START_BUTTON/config/start_button.pdl` (or `pieces/system/start_button_config.pdl`)

```
SECTION      | KEY                | VALUE
----------------------------------------
META         | piece_id           | start_button_config
META         | version            | 1.0

STATE        | scan_root          | ..
STATE        | scan_depth         | 2
STATE        | require_project_pdl| 0
STATE        | require_button_sh  | 1
STATE        | skip_names         | *.START_BUTTON,.claude,#.notes,#.DOX,#.haiku+,#.w8,#.media-library,net,exchange,xer,-1.metatree,404.architecture-bible,drag-drop-test,&.widgits
STATE        | skip_prefix        | #.,.
STATE        | launch_action      | run
STATE        | label_mode         | dir_name
STATE        | sort               | alpha
STATE        | max_entries        | 64
```

**Notes**

- `scan_root=..` means parent of `*.START_BUTTON` = house root (44.xyz). Change later to `projects` or an absolute path without code changes.  
- `require_project_pdl=0` + `require_button_sh=1` for v1 (discover runnables now).  
- When house projects gain proper `project.pdl` cards, flip `require_project_pdl=1` for pure TPMOS gate.  
- Nested apps (`0.user-pal/00.login-signup`) appear if `scan_depth>=2` and have `button.sh`.

Optional later fields on **per-project** `project.pdl`:

```
META | project_id    | agy-editor
META | display_name  | AGY Text Editor
META | entry_cmd     | ./button.sh run
META | entry_layout  | (only if in-process LOAD_PROJECT)
META | hide_from_loader | 0
```

---

## 5. Architecture (house pal shape)

```
*.START_BUTTON/
  button.sh
  scripts/build.sh
  default_op.txt
  config/start_button.pdl          # scan_root etc
  pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal
  system/                          # copy from login/editor: prisc+x, keyboard, renderer, chtpm_parser_pal
  ops/
    start_scan.c                   # TPMOS update_loader_pdl + write_gui_state parity
    start_compose_frame.c          # chrome + status into view.txt
    start_menu_input.c             # KEY:n / RUN: dispatch
  pieces/chtpm/layouts/loader.chtpm
  projects/start-button/pieces/loader/piece.pdl   # regenerated METHOD list
  pieces/system/... session state
  test-harn-same/                  # inject keys, capture frames
  proof/
```

### Signal flow (same as editor)

```
keyboard → history → chtpm_parser_pal
  → numbered buttons from ${piece_methods} or ${project_list}
  → KEY:n / RUN:name → interact_relay
  → start_menu_input → start_scan (if refresh) / launch
  → start_compose_frame → view.txt → frame_changed → renderer
```

### Layout (parity with TPMOS loader.chtpm)

```xml
<panel time_reactive=\"true\">
  <module>system/prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal</module>
  <interact src=\"pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt\" />
  <text label=\"${game_map}\" /><br/>
  ${piece_methods}
  <text label=\" R refresh catalog | Ctrl+C quit\" /><br/>
</panel>
```

**Compose** draws the PIECEMARK / HOUSE LOADER chrome + last_message + scan_root into `${game_map}`.  
**piece_methods** (from regenerated `loader` piece.pdl) is the numbered project list — same mechanism as login Switch: rows and editor NEW/CLEAR.

**Why piece_methods over inline project_list markup?**  
House chtpm already loads METHODs as `KEY:n` buttons via `load_dynamic_methods`. That is proven in login/editor. We can still *also* write a `project_list` var for visual parity if needed; v1 prefers piece_methods for less parser-path novelty.

### Regenerated catalog PDL (TPMOS loader.pdl shape)

```
METHOD | 102.editor-...     | RUN:102.editor-...
METHOD | 041.pal-forum...   | RUN:041.pal-forum...
METHOD | 0.user-pal/.../00.login-signup | RUN:0.user-pal/.../00.login-signup
...
METHOD | Refresh catalog    | REFRESH
METHOD | Quit               | EXIT
```

`start_scan` walks `scan_root` with depth/filter from config, sorts, rewrites this PDL, bumps screen.

### Launch semantics (v1)

On `RUN:<relpath>`:

1. Resolve `scan_root/relpath/button.sh`.  
2. If missing → `last_message=No button.sh`.  
3. If present → write `pieces/system/last_launch.txt` and  
   - **v1a (safe):** `last_message=Launch queued: …` + optional `setsid`/background `./button.sh run` from that dir **only if** env `START_BUTTON_DO_LAUNCH=1` (harness stays off).  
   - **v1b (interactive default for humans):** background launch when run from a real TTY session.  
4. Do **not** tear down the loader session by default (user can launch multiple / return). Optional later: kill loader on launch.

Mirror TPMOS `LOAD_PROJECT` only for apps that declare `META.entry_layout` **and** share this process — out of scope for v1 unless a pure-chtpm toy is added.

---

## 6. Discovery algorithm (start_scan)

```
root = realpath(config.scan_root)   # default ..
for each dir under root up to scan_depth:
  if name matches skip_names / skip_prefix: continue
  if require_button_sh and no button.sh: continue
  if require_project_pdl and no project.pdl: continue
  label = project.pdl display_name || dir basename
  rel = relative path from root
  append (label, rel)
sort alpha by label
write piece.pdl METHOD rows
```

**Depth 1** catches top-level apps (`102.editor`, `041.pal-forum`, …).  
**Depth 2** catches `0.user-pal/00.login-signup`, `0.user-pal/01.avatar-creation`, zoo subprojects.

Skip harness-only trees (`test-harn-same`) always.

---

## 7. Implementation phases

### Phase A — Scaffold + scan + list (first ship)

- Copy system sources from `102.editor` / login.  
- `config/start_button.pdl` with `scan_root=..`.  
- `start_scan`, `start_compose_frame`, `start_menu_input`.  
- Layout + pal loop + button.sh session isolation.  
- Harness: boot → frame shows LOADER chrome + at least N known apps (`102.editor`, `0.user-pal` children or forum).  
- Selecting a row only sets message + `last_launch.txt` (no process spawn in harness).

### Phase B — Real launch

- On RUN, `chdir` + `./button.sh run` in background with session hygiene.  
- Optional compile-if-missing.  
- Document: launcher and child both need terminals / background rules.

### Phase C — Stronger PDL cards

- Add/normalize `project.pdl` on house apps (`display_name`, `entry_cmd`).  
- Flip config to prefer PDL gate.  
- Optional `hide_from_loader`.

### Phase D — Extras (later)

- Refresh key without restart.  
- Filter / search cli_io.  
- \"Back\" if ever embedded in a larger OS.  
- True `LOAD_PROJECT` for monorepo chtpm children.

---

## 8. Harness proof (Phase A)

`test-harn-same/scenarios/demo_loader_list.sh`:

1. Boot START_BUTTON session.  
2. Assert frame contains loader chrome + `scan_root` echo.  
3. Assert at least one known dir label appears (e.g. substring `102.editor` or `pal-forum` / `login`).  
4. Focus first project method, Enter → `last_message` / `last_launch.txt` records RUN target.  
5. Focus Refresh → rescan still lists.  
6. Dump proof frames under `proof/harness-*`.

---

## 9. Explicit non-goals (v1)

- Replacing TPMOS orchestrator or embedding all apps in one process.  
- Auto-scanning the entire Desktop.  
- Implementing full file browser / FILE MENU.  
- Renaming every house folder.  
- GL desktop launch path.

---

## 10. Risk notes

| Risk | Mitigation |
|------|------------|
| Emoji / special chars in dir names | Always quote paths; store relpath in PDL carefully |
| Launch steals TTY from loader | Background child; harness never spawns |
| Too many / junk dirs | skip_names + require_button_sh |
| piece_methods KEY index off-by-one | Same formula as login/editor (`method_idx` starts 2) |
| Session isolation | Symlink system/ops; private pieces/sessions |

---

## 11. Recommended first slice after approval

1. Scaffold `*.START_BUTTON` like `102.editor`.  
2. Implement `start_scan` + compose list UI.  
3. Menu select writes `last_launch.txt` (no spawn).  
4. Harness frames green.  
5. Then Phase B human launch.

---

## 12. One-sentence thesis

**Build a house-local Piecemark Loader: config PDL points at catalog root (default `..`), scan regenerates a METHOD catalog like TPMOS `loader.pdl`, UI is pal-native chtpm+compose, and select runs that project\'s `button.sh` instead of same-process `LOAD_PROJECT`.**
"""
out.write_text(content)
out2.write_text(content)
print("wrote", out, "bytes", out.stat().st_size)
print("wrote", out2, "bytes", out2.stat().st_size)

## Assistant

## Investigation summary (TPMOS loader)

The menu you pasted is **generated**, not hand-authored.

In `1.TPMOS_c_+rmmp.0103.0001`:

1. **`loader_module.c`** scans `projects/`
2. Keeps only dirs with **`project.pdl`**
3. Rewrites **`loader.pdl`** as:
   - `METHOD | <name> | LOAD_PROJECT:<name>`
4. Writes **`${project_list}`** markup into gui_state (numbered buttons)
5. **`loader.chtpm`** is just chrome + `${project_list}` + Back
6. Activate → parser **`LOAD_PROJECT:`** → read `entry_layout` from that project’s PDL → **same-process** layout switch

So the important pattern is: **config + scan + regenerate PDL catalog + render as methods**, not “grep every shell forever.”

---

## House reality

Under 44.xyz, apps are **sibling folders** with their own `button.sh run` (editor, login, forum, mutaclsym…). They are **not** one shared TPMOS `projects/` process.

So for START_BUTTON:

| TPMOS | Our launcher |
|--------|----------------|
| scan `projects/` | scan **configurable root** (default `..` = house `./`) |
| gate: `project.pdl` | gate: `project.pdl` **or** `button.sh` (config flag) |
| `LOAD_PROJECT` in-process | **`RUN:relpath` → `button.sh run`** (own session) |
| loader_module daemon | house pal ops (like 102.editor) |

---

## Plan written

Both copies:

- `*.START_BUTTON/start-button-plan.md`
- `*.START_BUTTON/*.start-button-plan.md`

### Thesis
**House-local Piecemark Loader:** config PDL sets catalog root (`scan_root=..`), scan regenerates a METHOD catalog like TPMOS `loader.pdl`, UI is pal-native (chtpm + compose + methods), select runs that project’s `button.sh` — not same-process `LOAD_PROJECT`.

### v1 slice
1. Scaffold `*.START_BUTTON` like editor/login  
2. `config/start_button.pdl` with `scan_root=..`, depth, skip lists  
3. `start_scan` regenerates loader piece.pdl  
4. Select → write `last_launch.txt` (harness-safe; real spawn = Phase B)  
5. Harness frames: chrome + list contains known apps + select proof  

### Later
Phase B real launch · Phase C prefer `project.pdl` cards · Phase D refresh/filter/true LOAD_PROJECT for monorepo kids  

---

When you say go, we start **Phase A** (list + select marker + harness), same discipline as the editor.

---

## 0. Product shape — pre-screen of categories

START_BUTTON is **not** a flat dump of every `button.sh` on day one.
It opens as a **category pre-screen**, then each category is its own
list screen (TPMOS `os.chtpm` → Project Loader / App Store pattern).

```
+===========================================+
|           H O U S E   L O A D E R         |
|                                           |
|  [>] 1. System                            |
|  [ ] 2. Widgets                           |
|  [ ] 3. Apps                              |
|  [ ] 4. App Store                         |
|  [ ] 5. Quit                              |
+===========================================+
```

| Category   | Catalog root (config)     | Meaning |
|------------|---------------------------|---------|
| **System** | `scan_root` = house `./` (`..` from START_BUTTON) | House **system programs** living as sibling dirs under `./` that expose `button.sh` (editor, login, forum, mutaclsym, …). **Exclude** widget tree, apps tree, app-store tree, and the launcher itself — even if they have `button.sh`. |
| **Widgets**| `widgets_root` = `../&.widgits` | System-installed widgets. Empty / sparse today (`file-explorer` stub). |
| **Apps**   | `apps_root` = `../@.apps` | **Installed** user apps. Empty for now. |
| **App Store** | `store_root` = `../@.app-store` | Catalog of apps **available to install** (not yet run). Empty for now. |

Config PDL owns all four roots so paths can move later without code rewrites.

### Why not one mega-list of every button.sh?

- System / widgets / apps / store are **different install lifecycles**.
- Widgets and store packages must not pollute "system programs".
- Matches TPMOS mental model: OS menu → Project Loader vs App Store.

---

## 1. Prefer chtpm href + dynamic methods (no hard menu tree)

### Pre-screen = static **href** buttons (TPMOS / avatar / forum)

```xml
<!-- home.chtpm -->
<button label="System"    href="pieces/chtpm/layouts/system.chtpm" />
<button label="Widgets"   href="pieces/chtpm/layouts/widgets.chtpm" />
<button label="Apps"      href="pieces/chtpm/layouts/apps.chtpm" />
<button label="App Store" href="pieces/chtpm/layouts/store.chtpm" />
```

Parser already switches `current_layout` on Enter for `href` (same process,
cleanup_module + re-parse). **No custom nav hardcode.**

### Section lists = regenerated **`${piece_methods}`** (TPMOS loader.pdl + house login)

Each section layout:

```xml
<module>system/prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal</module>
<interact src="pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt" />
<text label="${game_map}" />
${piece_methods}
<button label="Back" href="pieces/chtpm/layouts/home.chtpm" />
```

`start_scan` rewrites:

```
projects/start-button/pieces/system/piece.pdl
projects/start-button/pieces/widgets/piece.pdl
projects/start-button/pieces/apps/piece.pdl
projects/start-button/pieces/store/piece.pdl
```

with rows like:

```
METHOD | 102.editor-... | RUN:../102.editor-...
METHOD | 041.pal-forum... | RUN:../041.pal-forum...
```

`load_dynamic_methods` already turns METHOD rows into `KEY:n` buttons.
Menu input only handles `RUN:` / `INSTALL:` / `REFRESH` — not drawing.

### Bridge after href (avatar lesson)

`${piece_methods}` follows `active_target_id` in `state.txt`, **not** the
layout path alone. Pure href does not set that.

**Tight fix:** idle tick in `start_menu_input` (key 0):

1. Read `pieces/display/current_layout.txt`
2. Map `.../system.chtpm` → piece `system` (etc.)
3. If `active_target_id` differs → `write_chtpm_bridge(piece)` + rescan that section
4. Clear stale `interact_relay` on layout change (avatar pattern)

So navigation stays **href-native**; methods stay **PDL-native**.

---

## 2. Same terminal window launch (functionally tight)

Goal: pick an entry → that program owns **this** TTY; when it exits,
return to the loader.

**Not** background `&` (steals nothing but also never "takes" the window).
**Not** nested raw-mode (keyboard_input still holding the TTY).

### Handoff loop (button.sh owns the TTY)

```
button.sh run:
  loop:
    start renderer + chtpm (+ module)
    keyboard_input          # blocks; raw mode
    kill session children
    if handoff_launch.txt exists:
        target=$(cat handoff_launch.txt); rm it
        (cd "$target" && ./button.sh run)   # SAME TTY, foreground wait
        # child exited → loop back into loader
    else:
        break   # real quit
```

### How selection triggers handoff

`RUN:relpath` in menu_input:

1. Resolve `button.sh` under catalog root + relpath  
2. Write absolute path (or dir) to `pieces/system/handoff_launch.txt`  
3. Write `pieces/system/quit_request.txt` so **keyboard_input yields**  
4. keyboard_input exits → shell continues handoff loop  

Requires a **tiny local patch** to START_BUTTON’s `keyboard_input.c`:
poll `quit_request.txt` / `handoff_launch.txt` (select timeout or
non-blocking check) and break — same as quit, without Ctrl+C.

No pipes required. No second TTY. One foreground process at a time.

### Store vs RUN

| Section | METHOD verb | v1 behavior |
|---------|-------------|-------------|
| system / widgets / apps | `RUN:path` | handoff → `button.sh run` |
| app store | `INFO:id` or later `INSTALL:id` | message only ("not installed"); install later |

---

## 3. Discovery rules (per section)

Shared: sort alpha, skip `#.*` / `.*` / `test-harn*`, max_entries from config.

| Section | Root | Include | Exclude |
|---------|------|---------|---------|
| system | `scan_root` (`..`) | dirs with `button.sh` (depth 1–2) | `*.START_BUTTON`, `&.widgits`, `@.apps`, `@.app-store`, notes/docs/net/exchange/metatree/architecture-bible, harness-only |
| widgets | `widgets_root` | child dirs (optional `button.sh` / `project.pdl`) | — |
| apps | `apps_root` | child dirs with launch entry | — |
| store | `store_root` | child dirs / package cards | do not RUN; list only |

`require_project_pdl` remains a config flag (default 0 for system until cards exist).

---

## 4. Architecture

```
*.START_BUTTON/
  button.sh                 # session + same-TTY handoff loop
  config/start_button.pdl   # roots + skip lists
  pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal
  system/                   # prisc+x, renderer, chtpm, keyboard (+ yield patch)
  ops/
    start_scan.c            # regenerate section piece.pdl files
    start_compose_frame.c   # chrome per section into view.txt
    start_menu_input.c      # idle bridge + RUN handoff + REFRESH
  pieces/chtpm/layouts/
    home.chtpm              # category href pre-screen
    system.chtpm            # ${piece_methods} + Back href
    widgets.chtpm
    apps.chtpm
    store.chtpm
  projects/start-button/pieces/{home,system,widgets,apps,store}/piece.pdl
  test-harn-same/
  proof/
```

### Signal flow

```
home href → system.chtpm
  idle: bridge active_target_id=system, start_scan(system)
  ${piece_methods} = RUN rows
  Enter RUN:foo → handoff_launch.txt + quit_request
  keyboard yields → button.sh runs foo in same TTY
  foo exits → loader restarts (loop)
```

---

## 5. Phases

**A (now):** scaffold, config, home hrefs, scan system/widgets/apps/store lists,
compose, idle bridge, RUN writes handoff (harness may not spawn), harness frames.  
**B:** keyboard yield + button.sh handoff loop (same TTY live).  
**C:** project.pdl display names; store INSTALL; refresh.

---

## 6. Thesis

**Pre-screen of four categories via chtpm href; each section’s list is
dynamic `${piece_methods}` from scanned PDL (not hardcoded rows); launch
is a same-TTY handoff loop in button.sh (foreground child, then return) —
not background forks, not in-process LOAD_PROJECT for foreign apps.**
''')
print("design updated", p)

========================================================================
34. FRAME HISTORY AUDIT LOG + IDLE COMPOSE DISCIPLINE (2026-07-27)
========================================================================

FRAME HISTORY (audit):
  system/renderer.c (house copy) appends every painted frame to
  pieces/display/frame_history.txt with a `--- FRAME at <ts> ---`
  marker, and TRUNCATES that file when the renderer process starts
  (each new session). That is the canonical offline audit trail —
  same idea as scrolling `--- FRAME UPDATE ---` terminal history.

  Session-scoped apps (user-pal / START_BUTTON throwaway sessions)
  lose that file when the session dir is deleted. For durable audits,
  set PRISC_FRAME_HISTORY to an install-path log that button.sh
  truncates at the start of each top-level `run` (START_BUTTON does
  this at debug/frame_history.txt).

IDLE COMPOSE (no spam):
  Pal loops that call `menu_input 0` every tick MUST NOT grow a
  screen_changed / frame_changed marker on every idle call. Only
  recompose when information changed (catalog fingerprint, layout,
  last_message, real key). Reference: mutaclsym composes only after
  game_dispatch handles work; see pal-pitfalls PITFALL 48.

  Wrong:  key==0 → bump_screen() always
  Right:  key==0 → recompute; bump only if fingerprint/state differs

ONE VISIBLE FRAME WRITER (sec. 20) still applies: ops write view.txt;
chtpm owns current_frame.txt; renderer only reads and logs.
EOF

## Assistant

## Short answers

### Does this project have `frame_history`?
**Yes — house renderer already does.**  
`system/renderer.c` (START_BUTTON, login, mutaclsym, etc.):

- Appends every paint to `pieces/display/frame_history.txt` with `--- FRAME at <ts> ---`
- **Truncates that file when the renderer process starts** (each new session)

**START_BUTTON catch:** sessions are throwaway, so that file dies with the session. For a durable audit:

- `debug/frame_history.txt` under install  
- Cleared every `./button.sh run`  
- Filled via `PRISC_FRAME_HISTORY` (just wired)

Same pattern as mutaclsym’s `pieces/display/frame_history.txt` (there it lives on the durable project tree).

### Frame spam (user-pal / idle loops)
**Root cause:** `userpal_menu_input` on key `0` did:

```c
regenerate_login_pdl();
bump_screen();  // EVERY ~30ms tick → compose spam
```

That is **not** mutaclsym-style (compose only after real work).

**Fixed:** idle still regenerates the account list, but **only bumps** when `piece.pdl` fingerprint changes.

START_BUTTON idle was already gated (only on layout/bridge change).

### Docs
Added if missing:

| Doc | Entry |
|-----|--------|
| `#.haiku+/!.pal-pitfalls+1.txt` | **PITFALL 48** — idle must not unconditional bump; mutaclsym contrast; frame_history for proof |
| `#.haiku+/!.pal-standards+1.txt` | **§34** — frame_history audit + idle compose discipline |

---

### After this change
- **user-pal:** rebuild applied; idle should stay quiet unless accounts change  
- **START_BUTTON:** `./button.sh run` → empty then fill `*.START_BUTTON/debug/frame_history.txt` for audits  

If spam continues after a real keypress flood, that’s expected (nav keys still recompose); idle alone should not.
