from pathlib import Path
p = Path("&.widgits/file-menu")
p.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
# also house-level pointer if useful
text = r'''# widget+plan.txt — File Menu Widget + Running-Process Registry
# House: 44.xyz  |  First widget: &.widgits/file-menu  (alias intent: %.widgets/file-menu)
# Primary consumer: 102.editor-... (AGY editor buffer / file_path)
# Pattern kin: #.drag-drop-test (file-mediated IPC), xyzfs session trees
# Status: PLAN ONLY — implement after phases agreed
# Date: 2026-07-27

================================================================================
0. THESIS
================================================================================

Widgets are **small pal-native programs** that do not own app logic; they
**send commands into a focused running program** through a shared, file-
mediated bus (same spirit as drag-drop pet import via exchange/ files and
window state — not sockets-first).

To know *who* can receive commands, every house program that wants widgets
registers itself while alive in the **user xyzfs process list**, and drops
itself on exit.

First widget: **file-menu** — NEW / SAVE / SAVE AS / LOAD FILE browser, plus
CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT. First integration target: **102.editor** buffer.

  editor  <—— shared document / cmd files ——>  file-menu widget
     |                                              |
     +—— both read process registry (xyzfs) ——+
     +—— optional: both open GL RGB windows (Step 2)

================================================================================
1. WHY THIS SHAPE (drag-drop kinship)
================================================================================

#.drag-drop-test / drag-drop-how2.md prove house IPC that is:

  - **File-backed** (exchange dir, config.pdl polls, state files)
  - **Process-decoupled** (two button.sh sessions, no shared memory)
  - **Ops-testable** (tk_* style harness: assert file, inject action)
  - **Optional visual surface** (GL windows for Xdnd; terminal still primary)

Widgets copy that contract:

  | Drag-drop pet import        | File-menu widget                    |
  |-----------------------------|-------------------------------------|
  | egg_window → gl_mirror      | file-menu → focused editor session  |
  | exchange/pet drop files     | xyzfs/.../widgets/inbox + document  |
  | windows poll config.pdl     | editor polls cmd file / mtime       |
  | drop_accepted flag          | cmd ACK / result in status file     |
  | harness ops assert import   | harness: SAVE writes disk, LOAD fills buffer |

================================================================================
2. RUNNING PROCESS REGISTRY (xyzfs)
================================================================================

Location (durable, per user — never session-only):

  <login-install>/xyzfs/users/<user_uuid>/home/runtime/
    processes.pdl          # catalog of live house processes
    processes/
      <pid>-<slug>/
        meta.pdl           # project_id, cwd, kind, started_at, capabilities
        document.txt       # optional shared document mirror (or path pointer)
        cmds/
          inbox.txt        # append-only command lines from widgets
          status.txt       # last ACK / error
        heart.beat         # mtime heartbeat (or heartbeat.txt with epoch)

Also machine-wide convenience (optional, for guest):

  <login-install>/xyzfs/session.pdl   # already exists — points at user_uuid
  <login-install>/xyzfs/runtime/      # guest fallback when not logged in

processes.pdl (regenerated on register/unregister, or scanned from dirs):

  SECTION | KEY | VALUE
  META    | piece_id | process_registry
  METHOD  | 102.editor-... (pid 12345) | FOCUS:12345
  METHOD  | 041.pal-forum... (pid ...)  | FOCUS:67890
  ...

meta.pdl fields (minimum):

  STATE | project_id     | agy-editor
  STATE | display_name   | 102.editor-...
  STATE | install_path   | absolute path to project root (session or install)
  STATE | session_path   | PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT if session-isolated
  STATE | kind           | editor | game | chat | other
  STATE | pid            | 12345
  STATE | capabilities   | file_document,text_buffer   (comma list)
  STATE | document_path  | relative or abs path of "active file"
  STATE | buffer_path    | pieces/system/editor_buffer.txt (under session)
  STATE | cmd_inbox      | .../cmds/inbox.txt
  STATE | started_at     | epoch
  STATE | widget_focus   | 0|1   (which process this widget instance targets)

Register / unregister:

  - button.sh run (any participating app): after session start, call
      ops/+x/runtime_register.+x
    trap EXIT/INT: runtime_unregister.+x
  - Heartbeat: main loop or lightweight timer touches heart.beat
  - Stale GC: widget or START_BUTTON scan drops entries with dead pid
    or heart older than N seconds

KPI (registry):
  - KPI-R1: editor run creates processes/<pid>-*/meta.pdl within 2s
  - KPI-R2: Ctrl+C editor removes registry entry (or GC marks dead)
  - KPI-R3: processes.pdl lists only live pids after GC
  - KPI-R4: guest path works without login (xyzfs guest-uuid tree)

================================================================================
3. FILE-MENU WIDGET — PRODUCT SURFACE
================================================================================

Install path:  &.widgits/file-menu/   (house spelling today: widgits)
Listed under START_BUTTON → Widgets (scan widgets_root).

3.1 Main screen (from file-flow / AGY frames)
---------------------------------------------

  ╔══════════════ F I L E   M E N U ════════════╗
  ║ ACTIVE: <focused project document path>     ║
  ║ FILE:   <truncated path display>             ║
  ║  1. NEW FILE                                ║
  ║  2. SAVE FILE                               ║
  ║  3. SAVE AS...                              ║
  ║  4. LOAD FILE...                            ║
  ║  5. CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT                    ║
  ║  status line                                ║
  ╚═════════════════════════════════════════════╝

Navigation: house pal-native (chtpm + piece_methods KEY:n, idle no spam).

3.2 CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT
------------------------

  - Method 5 → href (or request_layout) to focus.chtpm
  - focus screen lists **running** processes from xyzfs registry
    (not full house catalog — only live, capability-compatible if possible)
  - Enter on a row: set this widget instance's focused_pid / focused path
    in widget local state + optionally mark widget_focus in registry
  - Back href → file-menu main

KPI (focus):
  - KPI-F1: with 0 processes, focus screen shows empty + message
  - KPI-F2: with editor running, it appears on focus list
  - KPI-F3: selecting it updates ACTIVE: line on main file-menu
  - KPI-F4: Back returns without launching anything

3.3 NEW / SAVE / SAVE AS / LOAD
-------------------------------

Semantics when focus = 102.editor (capabilities include text_buffer):

  NEW FILE
    - write cmd to focused process inbox:  NEW
    - editor op clears editor_buffer.txt, resets file_path to untitled
    - optional: clear on-disk target only after user SAVE

  SAVE FILE
    - cmd: SAVE
    - editor (or widget if it has buffer path) writes buffer → document_path
    - if no path yet → treat as SAVE AS

  SAVE AS...
    - href → browser.chtpm MODE=SAVE_AS
    - user picks dir + name (cli_io name field + directory nav)
    - on confirm: cmd SAVE_AS:<abs-or-rel-path> OR widget writes file
      from buffer_path then cmd RELOAD_PATH:<path>

  LOAD FILE...
    - href → browser.chtpm MODE=LOAD (frames in file-flow.txt)
    - DIR listing, suggestions, BACK, [DIR]/[FIL] rows
    - LOAD FILE confirms selection
    - cmd: LOAD:<path>
    - editor replaces editor_buffer.txt contents from file, updates
      file_path in editor_state.txt, recomposes (idle-safe bump once)

Browser UI (from frame):

  MODE, DIR, SEARCH/FILE fields (cli_io optional Step 1.5)
  SUGGESTIONS (optional shallow)
  DIRECTORY CONTENTS with [<- BACK], [DIR], [FIL] + size
  LOAD FILE / CANCEL  (or SAVE / CANCEL in SAVE_AS mode)

KPI (file ops, editor focus):
  - KPI-E1: LOAD known file → editor buffer bytes match file (harness)
  - KPI-E2: type in editor, SAVE → disk file matches buffer
  - KPI-E3: NEW then SAVE AS path → new file exists, editor path updates
  - KPI-E4: LOAD while no focus → status "No focused project" (no crash)
  - KPI-E5: one frame pulse per completed action (no idle spam; pitfall 48)

================================================================================
4. SHARED DOCUMENT / COMMAND BUS
================================================================================

Prefer **paths in registry meta**, not copying huge buffers blindly.

Editor session already has:

  pieces/system/editor_buffer.txt
  pieces/system/editor_state.txt   # file_path=, cursor_pos=, last_message=

Registry meta.buffer_path / meta.document_path point at those (absolute
via session_path).

Command protocol (v1, line-oriented inbox):

  NEW
  SAVE
  SAVE_AS:<path>
  LOAD:<path>
  PING

Editor side (new small op or branch in editor_menu_input idle):

  - idle key 0: if inbox has lines, process one, write status.txt, bump once
  - LOAD: read file → editor_buffer.txt; set file_path; bump
  - SAVE: write buffer → file_path
  - SAVE_AS: set file_path; write buffer
  - NEW: empty buffer; file_path=docs/untitled.txt

Widget side:

  - never needs the editor TTY
  - only needs abs paths from registry + ability to append inbox
  - SAVE/LOAD can also be **widget-executed** if buffer_path is readable
    (widget writes disk from buffer_path) then PING editor to refresh path
    — dual mode; pick one in Phase B and stick to it

  Recommended v1: **editor executes cmds** (single writer of buffer);
  widget only enqueues + shows status. Matches "send commands to running
  programs."

ACK:

  status.txt:
    last_cmd=LOAD:/path
    result=ok|error
    message=Loaded 1234 bytes
    at=epoch

Widget polls status after enqueue (or single-shot read after sleep in
harness).

================================================================================
5. ARCHITECTURE (file-menu package)
================================================================================

&.widgits/file-menu/
  widget+plan.txt              # this file
  button.sh
  scripts/build.sh
  config/file_menu.pdl         # browser start dir, xyzfs root, gl on/off
  pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal
  system/                      # house copies: prisc+x, chtpm, keyboard, renderer
  ops/
    fm_compose_frame.c
    fm_menu_input.c
    fm_scan_dir.c              # directory listing → piece.pdl methods
    fm_enqueue_cmd.c           # append to focused inbox
    fm_focus_list.c            # regenerate focus piece.pdl from registry
    runtime_register.c         # may live in shared house ops later
    runtime_unregister.c
    runtime_gc.c
  pieces/chtpm/layouts/
    file_menu.chtpm            # main
    focus.chtpm                # CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT
    browser.chtpm              # LOAD / SAVE_AS
  projects/file-menu/pieces/{main,focus,browser}/piece.pdl
  test-harn-same/
  proof/
  debug/frame_history.txt      # cleared each run (PRISC_FRAME_HISTORY)

Editor deltas (102.editor):

  - runtime_register / unregister in button.sh
  - idle: process cmds/inbox.txt
  - config optional gl_window=0|1 (Step 2)

House / START_BUTTON:

  - Widgets category already scans &.widgits — file-menu appears when
    button.sh exists

================================================================================
6. STEP 1 — TERMINAL WIDGET + REGISTRY + EDITOR WIRING
================================================================================

### Phase 1.A — Process registry (foundation)
Goal: any run of 102.editor appears in xyzfs runtime list; exit cleans up.

Work:
  - Define paths under xyzfs/users/<uuid>/home/runtime/ (guest fallback)
  - runtime_register.+x / runtime_unregister.+x / runtime_gc.+x
  - Hook 102.editor button.sh run/trap
  - Heartbeat optional (pid liveness check may be enough for v1)

KPIs:
  - KPI-R1 … KPI-R4 (section 2)
  - Harness: start editor background-or-session, assert registry entry,
    kill, assert gone or GC'd

Exit: registry demo green without any widget UI.

### Phase 1.B — file-menu shell (UI only)
Goal: pal-native FILE MENU chrome matching frames; methods; no real IO yet.

Work:
  - Scaffold like START_BUTTON / editor
  - file_menu.chtpm + compose ACTIVE/FILE/status
  - Methods NEW/SAVE/SAVE AS/LOAD/CHANGE FOCUS (stubs → status messages)
  - Auto-compile on run (dev); frame_history audit; idle no spam

KPIs:
  - KPI-U1: harness home frame contains "F I L E   M E N U" + 5 methods
  - KPI-U2: each method Enter updates status once (not continuous spam)
  - KPI-U3: Ctrl+C clean quit

Exit: UI demo green.

### Phase 1.C — Focus project screen
Goal: href to list of running processes; select sets widget focus.

Work:
  - focus.chtpm + fm_focus_list regenerate piece.pdl from registry
  - Select writes widget state focused_pid + paths
  - Main compose shows ACTIVE: from focus

KPIs:
  - KPI-F1 … KPI-F4
  - Harness: register fake process dir → appears on focus list → select
    updates ACTIVE

Exit: focus demo green (can use fake registry entry without live editor).

### Phase 1.D — Command bus + editor LOAD/SAVE/NEW
Goal: widget commands change editor buffer/files.

Work:
  - Editor idle inbox consumer
  - Widget enqueue ops
  - Wire NEW/SAVE/LOAD (LOAD path typed or fixed harness path first)

KPIs:
  - KPI-E1 … KPI-E5
  - Harness scenario:
      1) spawn editor session (or mock buffer paths)
      2) widget LOAD fixture.txt
      3) assert editor_buffer.txt == fixture
      4) mutate buffer; SAVE; assert disk

Exit: end-to-end file path green without full browser polish.

### Phase 1.E — File browser (LOAD / SAVE AS)
Goal: directory navigation UI from file-flow frames.

Work:
  - browser.chtpm MODE=LOAD|SAVE_AS
  - fm_scan_dir → [DIR]/[FIL]/BACK methods
  - SAVE AS name field (cli_io) optional; can use fixed name method first
  - Cancel/Back

KPIs:
  - KPI-B1: open LOAD → listing includes known file under editor install
  - KPI-B2: enter [DIR] changes DIR: line
  - KPI-B3: BACK goes to parent
  - KPI-B4: LOAD FILE on [FIL] triggers LOAD cmd and buffer match
  - KPI-B5: SAVE AS creates new file at chosen path

Exit: browser demo green; matches frame intent.

### Phase 1.F — Polish + docs
  - GC stale processes on widget open
  - Capability filter (only show file_document processes on focus list)
  - Short README in &.widgits/file-menu/
  - Proof harness frames under proof/

KPI:
  - KPI-P1: full scenario demo_file_menu.sh ALL PASS
  - KPI-P2: frame_history idle growth < 5 frames/10s with no input

================================================================================
7. STEP 2 — OPTIONAL GL / RGB WINDOWS
================================================================================

Goal: file-menu (and editor) may open a GL window showing the same
frame (or a simplified chrome) via existing house rgb path — **config
flag, not required for widget correctness**.

Config (per project):

  STATE | gl_window     | 0|1
  STATE | gl_title      | File Menu
  STATE | gl_follow_rgb | 1

Work:
  - Reuse mutaclsym/gl-canvas patterns: compose_rgb_frame or gl_mirror
    of current_frame / rgb_frame.raw
  - button.sh starts gl_mirror only if gl_window=1
  - Editor same optional flag
  - Drag-drop later: widgets could be Xdnd sources/targets (out of scope
    until GL stable)

KPIs:
  - KPI-G1: gl_window=0 → no GL process
  - KPI-G2: gl_window=1 → window title appears (xdotool/find_window)
  - KPI-G3: terminal-only LOAD/SAVE still works with GL on
  - KPI-G4: kill path tears down GL with session

Exit: optional GL is a switch, not a rewrite.

================================================================================
8. NON-GOALS (v1)
================================================================================

  - Full OS file manager / multi-tab editor
  - Networked multi-machine process list
  - Installing widgets from app-store (later)
  - Xdnd of files from file-menu (after Step 2)
  - Replacing editor's own INTERACT typing (widget only file ops)

================================================================================
9. RISK REGISTER
================================================================================

  | Risk | Mitigation |
  |------|------------|
  | Session paths vanish when editor exits | Registry GC; widget shows dead focus message |
  | Two writers on editor_buffer | Editor sole buffer writer; widget only cmds |
  | xyzfs path when guest | Ensure guest-uuid tree like login |
  | Idle spam from browser refresh | Fingerprint dir listing; pitfall 48 |
  | Emoji paths | Always quote; realpath; abs paths in registry |
  | START_BUTTON launch same-TTY vs widget | Widget is separate button.sh; can run alongside editor in second TTY OR handoff — document: **second terminal** for widget while editor runs (same-TTY handoff would kill editor). |

IMPORTANT UX NOTE:
  START_BUTTON same-TTY handoff is wrong for "widget while app runs."
  Widgets are **parallel sessions** (second terminal or background
  module), talking via xyzfs — like drag-drop's two processes.

================================================================================
10. SUGGESTED IMPLEMENTATION ORDER (checklist)
================================================================================

  [ ] 1.A Registry + editor register/unregister + harness
  [ ] 1.B file-menu UI scaffold + harness frames
  [ ] 1.C Focus list href + select
  [ ] 1.D Cmd bus NEW/SAVE/LOAD
  [ ] 1.E Browser LOAD/SAVE AS
  [ ] 1.F Polish + full demo
  [ ] 2.x Optional GL flag for file-menu + editor

================================================================================
11. SUCCESS SNAPSHOT (done when)
================================================================================

A human can:

  1. Terminal A: ./102.editor/button.sh run  (registers process)
  2. Terminal B: ./&.widgits/file-menu/button.sh run
  3. CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT → select the editor
  4. LOAD FILE... → browse → load a text file → editor canvas shows it
  5. Edit in editor → SAVE FILE → disk updates
  6. frame_history / harness prove steps without spam

Step 2 add-on: both can show RGB windows with gl_window=1.

================================================================================
END widget+plan.txt
================================================================================
'''
(p / "widget+plan.txt").write_text(text)
# pointer at widgits root

## Assistant

## Plan written

**`&.widgits/file-menu/widget+plan.txt`**  
(House path is `&.widgits`, not `%.widgets` — plan notes both.)

Also: `&.widgits/README-widgets.txt` pointer.

---

### Core idea

Widgets **don’t replace apps** — they **send file-backed commands** to a **focused running process**, same spirit as **drag-drop** (exchange/files + two live processes, not sockets).

| Piece | Role |
|--------|------|
| **xyzfs runtime registry** | `users/<uuid>/home/runtime/processes/` while programs run |
| **file-menu** | NEW / SAVE / SAVE AS / LOAD + **CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT** |
| **102.editor** | First target: `editor_buffer.txt` + `file_path` via inbox cmds |
| **Shared bus** | Registry paths + `cmds/inbox.txt` / `status.txt` (editor executes cmds) |

**Focus flow:** main menu → href **focus** list of *live* processes → pick one → ACTIVE updates → file ops hit that project.

**Important:** widget runs in a **second session/terminal** while the editor stays up (START_BUTTON same-TTY handoff would kill the editor).

---

### Steps

**Step 1 — terminal only**  
1.A Registry · 1.B UI shell · 1.C Focus · 1.D Cmd bus LOAD/SAVE/NEW · 1.E Browser · 1.F Polish  

**Step 2 — optional GL**  
`gl_window=0|1` for file-menu + editor (reuse gl_mirror/rgb); not required for SAVE/LOAD.

---

### KPIs (sample)

- **R1–R4:** register/unregister/GC/guest  
- **F1–F4:** focus list + ACTIVE line  
- **E1–E5:** LOAD/SAVE/NEW match disk + buffer; no idle spam  
- **B1–B5:** browser dir nav + LOAD/SAVE AS  
- **G1–G4:** GL optional switch  

Full checklist and risks are in the plan file.

Say when to start **Phase 1.A** (process registry + editor hook).

## Tools

- Read: &.widgits/file-menu/widget+plan.txt (2-81)
- Execute: tail -40 '#.haiku+/!.pal-standards+1.txt'
grep -n 'GL\|widget\|ASCII\|renderer\|gl_window' '#.haiku+/!.pal-standards+1.txt' | head -30
 (Locate standards end and GL-related sections)
- Execute: cat >> '#.haiku+/!.pal-standards+1.txt' << 'EOF'

========================================================================
35. GL IS PRIMARY UI — ASCII/TERMINAL IS SECONDARY (HOUSE LAW, 2026-07-27)
========================================================================

THIS IS HOW USERS ACTUALLY INTERACT WITH HOUSE PROGRAMS AND WIDGETS.
Future agents MUST NOT design new apps as "terminal-first, GL optional."
The correct default stack is:

  PRIMARY:   GL / RGB window (chtpm_rgb_render, gl_mirror, project GL
             surface — same frame pipeline as ASCII, different output)
  SECONDARY: ASCII terminal renderer (nice for dev, harnesses, audit
             scrollback, headless CI)
  AUDIT:     frame_history.txt / rgb receipts (always file-backed)

Mutaclsym already behaves this way in practice (terminal + GL mirror).
Widgets and nested "menus opened by another program" go further: they
often run **GL-only** (no useful terminal UI at all).

35.1 Two launch profiles every program should support
-----------------------------------------------------

Every house project that can be a normal app OR a widget MUST expose
config (PDL / button.sh / env) for:

  PROFILE A — APP (default interactive run)
    - GL window ON (primary)
    - ASCII renderer ON or optional (secondary; dev may keep it)
    - keyboard_input may attach to terminal AND/OR GL key path
    - registers in xyzfs runtime process list

  PROFILE B — WIDGET (opened by another program, or "open as widget")
    - GL window ON (required — this is the user interface)
    - ASCII terminal renderer OFF:
        * do not take over the caller's TTY
        * system/renderer stdout → /dev/null (or never launch ASCII
          renderer)
        * still write current_frame.txt + rgb_frame.raw + frame_history
          for audit/harness
    - no second terminal window required for the user
    - still a separate process/session under the hood (file-mediated),
      just not a second interactive shell

Config sketch (per project config/*.pdl or pieces/system/ui_mode.pdl):

  STATE | ui_primary       | gl          # gl | ascii  (default gl)
  STATE | ascii_renderer   | 1|0         # 0 in widget profile
  STATE | gl_window        | 1|0         # 1 required for widget profile
  STATE | launch_profile   | app|widget
  STATE | widget_capable   | 1           # advertises "can be embedded/spawned"

button.sh should accept:

  ./button.sh run              # app profile
  ./button.sh run-widget       # or: run --widget / RUN_PROFILE=widget
  ./button.sh run-widget --focus-pid <pid>   # optional parent context

35.2 How an app opens a widget (example: editor → file-menu)
-----------------------------------------------------------

User in **editor** activates FILE menu method:

  1. Editor does NOT href to an in-process file browser as the long-term
     design (may stub early). Canonical: **spawn file-menu as widget**.
  2. Editor sets / passes launch_profile=widget (env or argv or a one-
     shot pdl drop under exchange/xyzfs).
  3. file-menu starts its own pal session + GL window only.
  4. ASCII path silenced (no TTY fight with editor).
  5. Commands still flow on the **shared document / cmd bus**
     (xyzfs runtime inbox) — same as plan in
     &.widgits/file-menu/widget+plan.txt.
  6. Closing the GL widget unregisters; editor keeps running.

This is the opposite of "open another terminal tab for the widget."
Parallel **processes** yes; parallel **user terminals** no.

35.3 ASCII remains valuable — do not delete it
----------------------------------------------

Keep ASCII renderer codepaths for:

  - harness / agent testing (tk_inject_key, frame_history text asserts)
  - SSH / headless without display
  - developer debugging of layout chrome

But product UX, demos, and widget surfaces are GL-first. Docs and plans
that say "Step 2 optional GL" for widgets are **wrong** under this law —
GL is Step 1 for user-facing widgets; ASCII-only is a dev fallback.

35.4 Pipeline (both surfaces, one compose)
------------------------------------------

  ops compose → view.txt
  chtpm compose_frame → current_frame.txt (+ renderer_pulse)
  optional compose_rgb_frame / chtpm_rgb_render → rgb_frame.raw
  system/renderer     → TTY (if ascii_renderer=1)
  system/gl_mirror    → GL window (if gl_window=1)

ONE VISIBLE FRAME WRITER (sec. 20) still applies to current_frame.txt.
RGB has its own writer rules (sec. 11). Widget mode does not invent a
third UI toolkit — it turns outputs on/off.

35.5 Process registry still required
------------------------------------

GL-primary does not remove xyzfs runtime registration. Widgets need to
know which app is focused; apps need to know which widgets they spawned.
See &.widgits/file-menu/widget+plan.txt and sec. 36 below.

35.6 Agent checklist (do not regress)
-------------------------------------

  [ ] New interactive project: gl_window defaults on for `run`
  [ ] widget_capable + run-widget path documented in button.sh help
  [ ] Widget spawn does not require a second human terminal
  [ ] ASCII can be disabled without breaking cmd bus / file state
  [ ] Harness can still assert via frame_history / files when ASCII off
  [ ] Do not design "widget = second TTY session the user must open"

EOF

# Pitfall entry
python3 << 'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path("#.haiku+/!.pal-pitfalls+1.txt")
t = p.read_text()
old = """=======================================================================
END OF PAL-PITFALLS
Add new pitfalls above this line as they are discovered.
========================================================================
"""
new = """========================================================================
PITFALL 49: Treating ASCII/terminal as primary and widgets as
            "open another terminal" — WRONG. GL is primary; widgets
            are GL-only spawn profiles (2026-07-27)
========================================================================

SYMPTOM: Plans and agents add Step 2 "optional GL" for widgets, or tell
users to open a second terminal for file-menu while the editor runs.
Product feels like a dev console farm, not an OS of windows.

ROOT CAUSE: Early house scaffolds (login, START_BUTTON, editor Phase 1)
shipped terminal-first because harnesses and agents are text-native.
That is a **dev convenience**, not the product architecture.

LAW (pal-standards sec. 35):
  - GL / RGB window = primary user surface
  - ASCII renderer = secondary (harness, headless, debug)
  - Widget launch profile = GL on, ASCII off (no second TTY)
  - App opens widget by spawning run-widget, not by asking for a shell

FIX:
  - button.sh: `run` (app) vs `run-widget` (GL-only)
  - ascii renderer stdout → /dev/null or skip process when widget
  - editor FILE menu → spawn &.widgits/file-menu as widget
  - update plans: GL is Step 1 for widgets, not Step 2

SEE: pal-standards §35, &.widgits/file-menu/widget+plan.txt

=======================================================================
END OF PAL-PITFALLS
Add new pitfalls above this line as they are discovered.
========================================================================
"""
if old not in t:
    raise SystemExit("end marker missing")
p.write_text(t.replace(old, new))
print("pitfalls ok")
PY
 (Append standards §35 and pitfall 49 for GL-primary widgets)

from pathlib import Path
p = Path("&.widgits/file-menu/widget+plan.txt")
text = r'''# widget+plan.txt — File Menu Widget + Running-Process Registry
# House: 44.xyz  |  First widget: &.widgits/file-menu
# Primary consumer: 102.editor (AGY buffer / file_path)
# Pattern kin: #.drag-drop-test (file-mediated IPC), mutaclsym GL mirror
# UI LAW: pal-standards §35 — GL PRIMARY, ASCII SECONDARY
# Status: PLAN — revised 2026-07-27 (GL-first widgets, no second TTY)
# Date: 2026-07-27

================================================================================
0. THESIS (REVISED)
================================================================================

**GL is how the user interfaces with the house.** ASCII/terminal is a
secondary surface (dev, harness, headless). Widgets are not "another
terminal you open" — they are **GL windows** spawned as a **widget
launch profile** of a program (ascii renderer off / nulled).

Widgets are small pal-native programs that **send commands** into a
**focused running program** through a file-mediated bus (drag-drop
kinship: exchange/xyzfs files, not sockets-first).

  Terminal A (optional): START_BUTTON or editor app (GL + maybe ASCII)
       |
       | user picks FILE in editor
       v
  file-menu process:  launch_profile=widget
                      gl_window=1   (PRIMARY UI — user clicks here)
                      ascii_renderer=0  (no TTY takeover, stdout /dev/null)
       |
       +—— cmds/inbox.txt ——> editor session (still running)
       +—— reads buffer_path / document_path from xyzfs registry

First widget: **file-menu** (NEW / SAVE / SAVE AS / LOAD + CHANGE FOCUS).
First host: **102.editor**.

Every future program should support the same **app vs widget** profiles
so any app can open any widget-capable project as a GL window.

See: #.haiku+/!.pal-standards+1.txt §35, PITFALL 49.

================================================================================
1. LAUNCH PROFILES (ALL PROGRAMS, GOING FORWARD)
================================================================================

  PROFILE app (default `./button.sh run`)
    gl_window=1          # primary
    ascii_renderer=0|1   # secondary; often 1 in dev, optional in product
    registers in xyzfs runtime

  PROFILE widget (`./button.sh run-widget` or RUN_PROFILE=widget)
    gl_window=1          # REQUIRED — this IS the UI
    ascii_renderer=0     # do not fight parent TTY; renderer not started
                         # or stdout→/dev/null
    still: session files, current_frame.txt, rgb_frame.raw, frame_history
    registers as widget process; may receive --focus-pid / parent ctx

Editor FILE menu:
  spawn:  &.widgits/file-menu/button.sh run-widget
  not:    "open a second terminal and run file-menu"

START_BUTTON same-TTY handoff remains valid for **replacing** the loader
with a full app. It is **not** the model for widgets-alongside-apps.

================================================================================
2. DRAG-DROP KINSHIP (IPC, NOT UI)
================================================================================

#.drag-drop-test proves **file-mediated, multi-process** IPC with GL
surfaces. Widgets reuse that IPC idea; UI law is stricter (GL primary).

  | Drag-drop                     | File-menu widget                      |
  |-------------------------------|----------------------------------------|
  | two processes + GL windows    | host app + widget process + GL window  |
  | exchange / drop files         | xyzfs runtime inbox + document paths   |
  | no shared memory              | same                                   |
  | harness asserts files         | harness asserts buffer/disk + optional rgb |

================================================================================
3. RUNNING PROCESS REGISTRY (xyzfs)
================================================================================

  <login>/xyzfs/users/<uuid>/home/runtime/
    processes.pdl
    processes/<pid>-<slug>/
      meta.pdl
      cmds/inbox.txt
      cmds/status.txt
      heart.beat

meta.pdl minimum:
  project_id, display_name, install_path, session_path, kind, pid,
  capabilities (file_document,text_buffer,widget_capable),
  document_path, buffer_path, cmd_inbox, gl_window_title,
  launch_profile (app|widget), started_at

Register on run / run-widget; unregister on exit; GC dead pids.

KPI-R1: editor app run → registry entry <2s
KPI-R2: exit removes entry (or GC)
KPI-R3: guest-uuid tree works
KPI-R4: widget spawn also registers (kind=widget, parent_pid optional)

================================================================================
4. FILE-MENU PRODUCT SURFACE (GL WINDOW CHROME)
================================================================================

Same information architecture as file-flow frames; **pixels in GL**, not
"must scroll a terminal."

Main:
  ACTIVE: <focused project document>
  FILE:   <path>
  1 NEW FILE | 2 SAVE | 3 SAVE AS... | 4 LOAD FILE... | 5 CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT
  status

CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT → focus layout (list **running** registry processes)
LOAD / SAVE AS → browser layout (DIR / [DIR] / [FIL] / BACK / confirm)

Input: GL key/mouse path (and/or same interact_relay from a non-TTY
keyboard source later). Harness may still inject keys via history files
without a visible ASCII renderer.

================================================================================
5. SHARED DOCUMENT / COMMAND BUS
================================================================================

Editor sole writer of editor_buffer.txt.
Widget enqueues cmds only:

  NEW | SAVE | SAVE_AS:<path> | LOAD:<path> | PING

Editor idle (or tick) consumes inbox → status.txt ACK.
Widget GL shows status; does not need editor TTY.

================================================================================
6. STEP 1 — GL WIDGET + REGISTRY + EDITOR (USER-FACING)
================================================================================

### Phase 1.A — Process registry
  runtime_register / unregister / gc; editor hooks.
  KPIs: R1–R4

### Phase 1.B — UI mode flags + run-widget on file-menu
  config: ui_primary=gl, gl_window, ascii_renderer, launch_profile
  button.sh run vs run-widget
  GL window shows FILE MENU chrome (rgb path); ASCII off in widget mode
  KPIs:
    KPI-G0: run-widget starts gl process, no interactive ASCII takeover
    KPI-G1: ascii_renderer=0 → no terminal frame spam / no TTY raw fight
    KPI-U1: GL or current_frame contains FILE MENU methods (harness via files)

### Phase 1.C — Focus project (GL)
  focus list from registry; set ACTIVE
  KPIs: F1–F4 (empty list, list editor, select updates ACTIVE, back)

### Phase 1.D — Cmd bus NEW/SAVE/LOAD with live editor
  KPIs: E1–E5 (load/save/new; no focus error; one pulse per action)

### Phase 1.E — File browser LOAD/SAVE AS
  KPIs: B1–B5

### Phase 1.F — Editor opens widget
  Editor method FILE → spawn file-menu run-widget (not in-process stub long-term)
  KPI-W1: from editor app, FILE opens GL file-menu without second terminal
  KPI-W2: LOAD from that widget updates editor buffer
  KPI-W3: closing widget leaves editor alive

### Phase 1.G — Polish
  GC, capability filter, proof harness, frame_history (files always)
  KPI-P1: demo_file_menu_widget.sh ALL PASS
  KPI-P2: idle frame markers stable (pitfall 48)

================================================================================
7. STEP 2 — APP PROFILE GL DEFAULT + HOUSE ROLL-OUT
================================================================================

Not "add GL someday" — **normalize all programs**:

  - 102.editor `run` defaults gl_window=1 (ASCII secondary)
  - START_BUTTON / login: document migration path to GL-primary
  - Standard button.sh flags copied into project templates
  - Optional: widget chrome theming, multi-widget z-order

KPIs:
  KPI-H1: editor run shows GL without extra flags
  KPI-H2: template/README for new projects lists app vs widget profiles
  KPI-H3: standards §35 cited in new project button.sh help

ASCII-only remains valid for harness-only CI (display=:none).

================================================================================
8. NON-GOALS (v1)
================================================================================

  - Requiring a second human terminal for widgets
  - Full desktop WM
  - Network process list
  - Xdnd file drag from file-menu (nice later; drag-drop already exists for pets)

================================================================================
9. RISKS
================================================================================

  | Risk | Mitigation |
  |------|------------|
  | Headless CI no display | harness uses files + optional xvfb; ascii optional |
  | Two GL + one keyboard | widget uses own input path; document focus |
  | Buffer dual-write | editor sole buffer writer |
  | Session path dies | registry GC; widget status "focus dead" |
  | Agents re-introduce TTY widgets | PITFALL 49 + §35 checklist |

================================================================================
10. SUCCESS SNAPSHOT
================================================================================

  1. User runs editor (GL primary).
  2. User activates FILE → file-menu **GL window** appears (no new TTY).
  3. CHANGE FOCUS if needed → editor selected.
  4. LOAD FILE browser in widget GL → editor buffer updates.
  5. Edit in editor → SAVE via widget → disk updates.
  6. Close widget GL → editor still running.
  7. Agents read frame_history / status files for proof.

================================================================================
11. DOC POINTERS FOR AGENTS
================================================================================

  #.haiku+/!.pal-standards+1.txt  §35 GL primary / widget profile
  #.haiku+/!.pal-pitfalls+1.txt   PITFALL 49
  #.haiku+/!.pal-standards+1.txt  §11 RGB, §20 one frame writer, §34 idle
  this file                       product + phases + KPIs

================================================================================
END widget+plan.txt
================================================================================
'''
p.write_text(text)

 (Revise widget plan for GL-first; update widgets README)

## Assistant

## Updated for future agents

### Standards — `#.haiku+/!.pal-standards+1.txt` **§35**
**GL is primary. ASCII/terminal is secondary.**

Every program should support two profiles:

| Profile | Command | GL | ASCII |
|---------|---------|----|--------|
| **app** | `./button.sh run` | on (primary) | optional (dev/harness) |
| **widget** | `./button.sh run-widget` | **required** | **off** (no TTY fight; stdout `/dev/null` or skip ASCII renderer) |

**Editor → FILE menu:** spawn file-menu as **widget** (GL window only).  
Not “open another terminal.”

Still one compose pipeline (`view.txt` → `current_frame` → optional `rgb_frame`); widget mode only toggles which outputs run.

### Pitfalls — **PITFALL 49**
Calling out the failure mode: treating ASCII as primary / widgets as second terminals. Points at §35 + the plan.

### Plan — `&.widgits/file-menu/widget+plan.txt`
Rewritten so:
- GL widgets are **Step 1** (user-facing), not optional Step 2  
- Parallel **processes** yes; parallel **user TTYs** no  
- Editor FILE → `run-widget` KPIs (W1–W3)  
- Step 2 = house-wide roll-out of app/widget profiles  

`&.widgits/README-widgets.txt` points agents at the same law.

---

**Bottom line for implementers:** harnesses can stay file/ASCII-friendly; product and widgets are **GL windows**. ASCII is a nice-to-have secondary surface, not the interface for file-menu or other widgets.

- Execute: cat >> '#.haiku+/!.pal-standards+1.txt' << 'EOF'

========================================================================
36. WHERE HARNESSES LIVE — PER-PROJECT vs CROSS-PROJECT (2026-07-27)
========================================================================

Agents forget this often. The house already has two clear patterns.
Do not invent a third without a reason.

36.1 Default: harness LIVES INSIDE the project under test
---------------------------------------------------------

Every pal-native project that has a GUI (or widget) gets:

  <project>/
    button.sh
    ops/ ...
    test-harn-same/          # preferred name (family standard)
      button.sh              # compile | demo | kill
      ops/                   # tk_* or project-specific small C ops
      scenarios/             # demo_*.sh sequences of ops
      README.txt             # why + how to run
    proof/                   # timestamped frame dumps from last demos
      harness-YYYYMMDD-.../

Canonical examples (copy shape, not necessarily files):

  044.pal-chat-irc…/test-harn-same/     # multi-user chat UX
  041.pal-forum…/test-harn-same/
  0.user-pal/00.login-signup/test-harn-same/
  0.user-pal/01.avatar-creation…/test-harn-same/
  102.editor-…/test-harn-same/
  *.START_BUTTON/test-harn-same/

Also seen: `test-harn/` (older / shorter name) — same idea
(0.a-z-pets-plan, 150.gl-canvas, 151.screen-rec). Prefer
**test-harn-same** for new work so agents can grep one name.

Methodology reference (key injection, multi-digit nav, frame asserts):

  #.haiku+/!.local-ux-testing-ai.txt

Architecture of the harness itself (thin button.sh → ops → scenarios):

  Same as the app: logic in small +x ops, scenarios are op sequences.
  See any test-harn-same/README.txt (chat or login) for the full rant
  on why bash-inlined inject logic is the wrong long-term shape.

36.2 YES — different projects get different harnesses
-----------------------------------------------------

| What you are proving                         | Where the harness goes              |
|----------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------|
| file-menu UI alone (chrome, browser, focus)  | &.widgits/file-menu/test-harn-same/ |
| editor INTERACT canvas alone                 | 102.editor-…/test-harn-same/        |
| login / signup alone                         | 0.user-pal/00.login-signup/test-harn-same/ |
| START_BUTTON category list                   | *.START_BUTTON/test-harn-same/      |

Each owns:
  - its own tk_* (or dd_*) ops copy or local variants
  - its own scenarios/
  - its own proof/ dumps
  - its own `button.sh demo` entry

Do **not** stuff editor canvas tests into the widget tree, or widget
browser tests into the editor tree, unless the scenario is truly about
the **integration** of both (next section).

36.3 Cross-project / multi-instance harnesses (the one people forget)
---------------------------------------------------------------------

When the feature under test **is the interaction between two (or more)
running projects**, the harness does **not** belong inside only one of
them. It lives as a **sibling house directory** (often `#.`-prefixed
tooling), with scenarios that start/stop both sides.

Canonical example:

  #.drag-drop-test/
    button.sh
    ops/          # dd_set_positions, dd_drag_drop, dd_check_import, …
    scenarios/    # test_basic_import.sh
    README.txt

  Proves: muchi-pals egg_window → mutaclsym gl_mirror pet import.
  Neither project alone owns that story.

Other multi-process examples living *inside* one repo when both
"instances" are the same app (two sessions of chat):

  044.pal-chat-irc…/test-harn-same/scenarios/demo_2user_chat.sh
  → still under chat's harness, because both sessions are that project.

Rule of thumb:

  - Same project, N sessions  → stay under that project's test-harn-same
  - Project A × Project B      → house-level `#.<feature>-test/` (or
    exchange/ harness), ops may call both button.sh paths

For file-menu **as widget of editor** (GL spawn + LOAD into buffer):

  Option A (preferred once both exist):
    &.widgits/file-menu/test-harn-same/scenarios/demo_editor_load.sh
    → scenario starts editor session + widget session, asserts files
    (widget owns the cross-story because the feature is "widget drives
    host"; editor keeps its own unit harness for canvas typing)

  Option B (if the cross-story grows to many hosts):
    #.widget-host-test/ or #.file-menu-integration-test/
    → same shape as #.drag-drop-test

Both are valid; pick A until a second host needs the same harness.

36.4 What every harness must still honor
----------------------------------------

  - Real key path when testing GUI: history KEY_PRESSED → chtpm
    (not secret shortcuts that skip the UI)
  - Assert files + frame_history / current_frame (ASCII optional under
    §35 widget/GL mode — assert buffer/disk/rgb/status files)
  - proof/ artifacts for agents to read without re-running
  - kill/cleanup that does not murder unrelated house projects
    (cwd-scoped kills where possible)
  - Idle/no spam (pitfall 48) so demos do not fill proof with noise

36.5 Agent checklist
--------------------

  [ ] Unit feature of one project? → <that project>/test-harn-same/
  [ ] Two different projects interacting? → #.<name>-test/ or host
      scenario under the feature's project with both button.sh paths
  [ ] proof/ next to the harness that produced it
  [ ] README names sibling examples (chat, login, drag-drop)
  [ ] Widget GL-only: still test via files/registry; do not require TTY

SEE ALSO: §35 GL primary; widget+plan.txt; !.local-ux-testing-ai.txt
EOF
echo "standards §36 appended"
# also short note in widget plan section 11
python3 << 'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path("&.widgits/file-menu/widget+plan.txt")
t = p.read_text()
needle = "================================================================================\n11. DOC POINTERS FOR AGENTS\n================================================================================"
insert = """================================================================================
10b. HARNESS PLACEMENT (this feature)
================================================================================

  Unit (file-menu chrome / browser / focus UI alone):
    &.widgits/file-menu/test-harn-same/
    proof/

  Unit (editor canvas alone — already exists):
    102.editor-…/test-harn-same/

  Integration (widget LOAD/SAVE against live editor):
    Prefer scenarios under file-menu/test-harn-same/ that spawn both
    (widget owns the cross-story). If many hosts appear later, promote
    to house-level #.file-menu-integration-test/ like #.drag-drop-test.

  Law for agents: pal-standards §36.

"""
if "10b. HARNESS" not in t:
    if needle not in t:
        raise SystemExit("needle missing")
    p.write_text(t.replace(needle, insert + needle))
    print("plan harness section added")
else:
    print("already had section")

## Assistant

## Where harnesses go

### Default: **inside the project under test**
```
&.widgits/file-menu/
  test-harn-same/     ← widget UI / browser / focus unit tests
    button.sh
    ops/              (tk_* or fm_*)
    scenarios/
    README.txt
  proof/              ← frame dumps from demos
```

Same pattern as:

- `0.user-pal/00.login-signup/test-harn-same/`
- `044.pal-chat-irc…/test-harn-same/`
- `102.editor-…/test-harn-same/`
- `*.START_BUTTON/test-harn-same/`

**Yes — each project/instance gets its own harness.** Editor canvas tests stay under the editor; file-menu chrome tests stay under the widget.

---

### Cross-project (the one people forget): **sibling house harness**

When the story is **A × B together**:

| Example | Location |
|---------|----------|
| muchi-pals egg → mutaclsym drop | **`#.drag-drop-test/`** (not inside either app alone) |
| two chat sessions | still under **chat** `test-harn-same` (same project, N sessions) |
| file-menu **drives** editor LOAD/SAVE | start under **`file-menu/test-harn-same/scenarios/`** that spawn both; promote to `#.file-menu-integration-test/` only if many hosts appear |

---

### Documented for agents

**`#.haiku+/!.pal-standards+1.txt` §36** — per-project vs cross-project, examples, checklist.  
**`&.widgits/file-menu/widget+plan.txt` §10b** — concrete paths for this feature.

**Rule of thumb:** one project’s feature → that project’s `test-harn-same/`. Two different projects interacting → `#.<feature>-test/` (drag-drop shape) or a scenario under the *feature owner* that starts both.

from pathlib import Path
from datetime import date
# today from user_info was 2026-07-28
today = "2026-07-28"

roadmap = r'''# WIDGETS ROADMAP — file-menu (focus-adaptive) + tile-picker + map-picker
# House: 44.xyz  |  &.widgits/
# Date: ''' + today + r'''
# Status: PLAN (extends file-menu/widget+plan.txt; does not replace cmd-bus proof)

================================================================================
0. THESIS
================================================================================

Widgets are GL-primary, file-mediated controllers of a **focused**
running program (xyzfs runtime registry). file-menu is **not** one UI
for all hosts: its browser and verbs **adapt to the focused project's
kind**.

  focus = agy-editor / text_buffer
    → text documents under user FS for that project
    → NEW / SAVE / SAVE_AS / LOAD single files (existing plan)

  focus = mutaclysm / game_world
    → **save slots** = named project dirs under user FS
    → NEW  = wipe live maps+entities (fresh world)
    → SAVE / SAVE_AS = snapshot live world → save dir
    → LOAD = restore save dir → live world (maps + entities)

Also planned:

  tile-picker/  — paint emoji tiles onto mutaclysm map cells
  map-picker/   — list maps in focused mutaclysm project; switch +
                  teleport xlector/hero there

User filesystem (xyzfs) is the durable home for **both** editor docs and
mutaclysm save slots — not only install-tree pieces/saves/.

UI law: pal-standards §35 (GL primary), §36 (harness placement).
Cmd-bus proof already green: %.harnesses/file-menu+editor/

================================================================================
1. USER FILESYSTEM (xyzfs) — EDITOR + MUTACLYSM
================================================================================

Canonical root (logged-in user; guest uses guest-uuid tree):

  <login-install>/xyzfs/users/<user_uuid>/home/
    projects/
      <project_id>/                 # e.g. mutaclysm | agy-editor
        meta.pdl                    # display_name, kind, capabilities
        docs/                       # EDITOR: text documents
          untitled.txt
          …
        saves/                      # MUTACLYSM: named save slots
          demo-project/             # seeded from install world (see §2)
            meta.pdl
            world_01/               # maps + entities snapshot
          my-slot-1/
            meta.pdl
            world_01/
        runtime/                    # optional live mirrors / last focus
          last_save.txt
          last_doc.txt

project_id mapping (registry meta.project_id / install slug):

  101.mutaclsym…     → mutaclysm   (kind=game_world)
  102.editor…        → agy-editor  (kind=text_buffer)

If not logged in: same layout under guest-uuid home (already used by
login-signup for wallets/avatars).

EDITOR (add if missing — was only "session buffer + path" before):

  - Default SAVE/LOAD roots: xyzfs/.../projects/agy-editor/docs/
  - file-menu when focus=editor browses that docs/ tree (not whole disk)
  - Session buffer still primary for typing; SAVE commits into user FS

MUTACLYSM:

  - Live working world stays under install/session:
      pieces/world_01/<map_id>/   (map.txt, furniture, hero, monsters, items…)
  - User-visible save slots live under xyzfs projects/mutaclysm/saves/<name>/
  - Install pieces/saves/save_N remains legacy; new path is user FS

KPI-UFS1: editor SAVE_AS without path defaults under user docs/
KPI-UFS2: mutaclysm SAVE creates xyzfs/.../saves/<name>/world_01/
KPI-UFS3: guest path works without login
KPI-UFS4: demo-project exists after first mutaclysm register/seed

================================================================================
2. DEMO-PROJECT SEED (mutaclysm)
================================================================================

Problem: NEW / wipe of live world must not lose the only playable content.

Action (once per user, or if demo-project missing):

  1. Resolve install mutaclysm live or template tree
       prefer: pieces/world_01/ if populated
       else:   pieces/world_01_template/
  2. Copy into:
       xyzfs/users/<uuid>/home/projects/mutaclysm/saves/demo-project/world_01/
  3. Write meta.pdl:
       STATE | name | demo-project
       STATE | kind | mutaclysm_save
       STATE | seeded_from | install world_01 | template
       STATE | read_only | 0   # or 1 if we want demo immutable; default 0
                              # with "restore demo" verb later

Also keep install world_01_template/ as factory source for hard reset.

KPI-D1: after seed, demo-project listing appears in file-menu mutaclysm mode
KPI-D2: wipe live world; LOAD demo-project restores maps+entities playable
KPI-D3: seed is idempotent (no clobber if demo-project already exists,
        unless force flag)

================================================================================
3. FILE-MENU — FOCUS = MUTACLYSM (adaptive profile)
================================================================================

When widget focus meta.kind == game_world / project_id mutaclysm:

Main chrome (same shell, different verbs):

  ACTIVE: mutaclysm  |  LIVE: pieces/world_01  |  SLOT: <last save name or (none)>
  1. NEW GAME          — wipe live maps+entities (re-init empty or from
                         factory template — product choice: empty vs
                         template; default **empty shell + prompt** or
                         **copy template**; plan default = reset from
                         world_01_template then optional clear entities)
  2. SAVE              — snapshot live → current slot (or force SAVE AS)
  3. SAVE AS…          — browser of user saves/ + name; write new slot
  4. LOAD GAME…        — browser of user saves/ dirs only (not raw files)
  5. CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT

Browser mode when mutaclysm:

  - ROOT fixed: xyzfs/.../projects/mutaclysm/saves/
  - List **dirs** as save slots (demo-project, my-slot-1, …)
  - No generic file picker of .txt (unless advanced toggle later)
  - LOAD confirms: copy slot/world_01 → live pieces/world_01 (atomic
    replace: write to world_01.tmp then swap)
  - SAVE AS: copy live pieces/world_01 → saves/<name>/world_01

Cmd bus verbs (mutaclysm consumer):

  NEW_GAME
  SAVE_GAME
  SAVE_GAME_AS:<slot_name>
  LOAD_GAME:<slot_name>
  PING

Mutaclysm side (new ops or extend save_game.c):

  - muta_save_slot.+x <user_saves_root> <slot_name>
  - muta_load_slot.+x <user_saves_root> <slot_name>
  - muta_new_game.+x  [from_template|empty]
  - muta_seed_demo.+x <user_saves_root>   # copy current/template → demo-project

What "maps and entities" means (install shape today):

  pieces/world_01/
    map_start/ map_02/ map_gen_01/ building_01_gf/ …
      map.txt furniture.txt transitions.txt state.txt
      hero/ monsters/ items/ …   # entities as piece dirs + state.txt

Save slot is a full tree copy of world_01 (same as existing save_game
cp -r pattern, but destination = user FS save name folder).

Phases (file-menu mutaclysm):

  M1. User FS layout + seed demo-project from install world
  M2. muta_save_slot / muta_load_slot / muta_new_game ops
  M3. file-menu focus profile switch (editor vs mutaclysm) in compose
  M4. Browser lists save dirs only; LOAD/SAVE AS wired
  M5. Harness under %.harnesses/file-menu+mutaclysm/ (or extend multi)

KPIs:

  KPI-M1: demo-project present under user saves after seed
  KPI-M2: SAVE_AS "t1" creates world_01 under saves/t1/
  KPI-M3: mutate live map; LOAD t1 restores prior map.txt bytes
  KPI-M4: NEW_GAME clears or resets live; demo-project still loadable
  KPI-M5: focus=editor still shows text-file browser (no regression)

================================================================================
4. TILE-PICKER WIDGET  (&.widgits/tile-picker/)
================================================================================

Purpose: paint one emoji/glyph onto a mutaclysm map cell.

UI (GL widget window):

  - Palette of emojis/glyphs (from mutaclysm registry / furniture /
    terrain tables — source TBD: pieces/registry or data files)
  - User **clicks emoji** → selected tile
  - User **clicks a cell** on the focused mutaclysm level (GL map /
    interact map control) → place tile

Protocol:

  1. tile-picker focus must be mutaclysm session with map edit capability
  2. Selection: write selected_glyph to widget state + optional
     mutaclysm gui_state / paint_brush.txt
  3. Placement: either
       A) mutaclysm map INTERACT already places brush glyph (preferred
          if map control exists), or
       B) cmd bus: PLACE_TILE:<map_id>:<x>:<y>:<glyph>
          → mutaclysm op place_tile / patch map.txt UTF-8-safe
  4. Recompose map (one frame pulse; no idle spam)

Dependencies:

  - Focused mutaclysm running + registered
  - Map identity (current_map / map_start etc.) known to session
  - Prefer reuse OP-ED / place_tile patterns if present

Phases:

  T1. Palette UI (static emoji list from config or registry dump)
  T2. Brush state file shared with mutaclysm
  T3. Place on click (GL hit-test or ASCII map coords via cmd)
  T4. Harness: set brush + PLACE_TILE → map.txt cell changes

KPIs:

  KPI-T1: select emoji updates brush state file
  KPI-T2: PLACE_TILE changes map.txt at (x,y) to that glyph
  KPI-T3: wrong focus (editor) → status error, no crash
  KPI-T4: GL click path (Step after terminal/cmd proof)

================================================================================
5. MAP-PICKER WIDGET  (&.widgits/map-picker/)
================================================================================

Purpose: list all maps in the focused mutaclysm **live** project
(world_01/* map dirs), switch current map, and **teleport xlector/hero**
to that map (sensible spawn / last pos / map entry).

UI (GL):

  ACTIVE: mutaclysm | current map_start
  List:
    [ ] map_start
    [ ] map_02
    [ ] map_gen_01
    [ ] building_01_gf
    …
  Enter / click → SWITCH_MAP:<map_id>

Protocol:

  SWITCH_MAP:<map_id>
    - mutaclysm sets current_map / session map id
    - moves hero + xlector (selector) onto that map's piece tree
      (state.txt map coords — match existing transition/warp logic)
    - recomposes

Discovery:

  scan pieces/world_01/*/ for dirs that look like maps (map.txt or
  state.txt present); sort alpha.

Phases:

  P1. Scan + list maps in widget methods
  P2. SWITCH_MAP op + hero/xlector teleport
  P3. Highlight current map; refresh on world load
  P4. Harness: switch map_start → map_02; assert current_map + hero path

KPIs:

  KPI-P1: list includes all live world_01 map dirs
  KPI-P2: switch updates session current_map
  KPI-P3: hero/xlector state lives under target map after switch
  KPI-P4: after LOAD_GAME different slot, list refreshes

================================================================================
6. IMPLEMENTATION ORDER (suggested)
================================================================================

  [ ] 0.  User FS dirs for agy-editor/docs + mutaclysm/saves (provision)
  [ ] 1.  Seed demo-project from install world / template
  [ ] 2.  muta_save_slot / load_slot / new_game ops
  [ ] 3.  file-menu focus profile: mutaclysm save-dir browser
  [ ] 4.  %.harnesses/file-menu+mutaclysm demo (SAVE/LOAD/NEW + demo intact)
  [ ] 5.  map-picker list + SWITCH_MAP + xlector teleport
  [ ] 6.  tile-picker brush + PLACE_TILE (cmd first, GL click second)
  [ ] 7.  GL chrome for all three widgets (run-widget)
  [ ] 8.  Editor user-docs browser path (file-menu editor profile)

Editor file-menu path already partially proven (cmd bus). Mutaclysm path
is the new work. tile-picker and map-picker assume mutaclysm focus +
live world.

================================================================================
7. HARNESS PLACEMENT (§36)
================================================================================

  Unit file-menu UI:     &.widgits/file-menu/test-harn-same/
  Unit tile-picker:      &.widgits/tile-picker/test-harn-same/
  Unit map-picker:       &.widgits/map-picker/test-harn-same/
  Editor alone:          102.editor-…/test-harn-same/
  Editor × file-menu:    %.harnesses/file-menu+editor/     (DONE green)
  Mutaclysm × file-menu: %.harnesses/file-menu+mutaclysm/  (TODO)

8. NON-GOALS (this roadmap)
================================================================================

  - Full RPG maker / multi-user cloud saves
  - Arbitrary disk browser outside user project trees (by default)
  - Replacing mutaclysm in-game "save" bar immediately (may dual-path)

================================================================================
9. OPEN QUESTIONS (product)
================================================================================

  Q1: NEW_GAME = empty world vs copy world_01_template?
  Q2: Is demo-project read-only, or overwriteable with "Reset demo"?
  Q3: tile-picker paints terrain map.txt only, or also furniture/entities?
  Q4: map-picker teleport = hero only, xlector only, or both?

Defaults for planning until decided:
  Q1 → reset live from template (not zero-byte maps)
  Q2 → overwriteable; seed only if missing
  Q3 → map.txt terrain/glyph first; furniture later
  Q4 → both hero and xlector/selector

================================================================================
END WIDGETS ROADMAP
================================================================================
'''

Path("&.widgits/WIDGETS_ROADMAP.txt").write_text(roadmap)

User can:
  - see running processes (from xyzfs runtime registry + live PID check)
  - FOCUS one (for file-menu / other widgets)

Full plan: &.widgits/WIDGETS_ROADMAP.txt §PROC
Standards: XYZOS_README.md, pal-standards §35–36
Harness (later): %.harnesses/proc-monitor+runtime/ or multi with file-menu
""")

================================================================================
5b. PROC-MONITOR WIDGET  (&.widgits/proc-monitor/)
================================================================================

Purpose: **system tray / task manager for XYZOS processes** — not Linux
`ps` cosplay, but the house runtime list: anything that registered
(editor, mutaclysm, widgets, headless harness sessions, future @apps).

UI (GL primary, ASCII secondary/off in run-widget):

  ╔════════════ P R O C   M O N I T O R ════════════╗
  ║  RUNNING (N)                                     ║
  ║  [>] 1. mutaclysm     pid 1234  GL  app          ║
  ║  [ ] 2. agy-editor    pid 1235  GL  app          ║
  ║  [ ] 3. file-menu     pid 1236  GL  widget       ║
  ║  [ ] 4. (headless) harn-fm-…  pid …  no-window   ║
  ║                                                  ║
  ║  1. FOCUS          → set widget_focus for others ║
  ║  2. KILL           → SIGTERM then SIGKILL        ║
  ║  3. SOFT QUIT      → quit_request / quit_flag    ║
  ║  4. REFRESH        → re-scan registry + reaping  ║
  ║  5. OPEN / RAISE   → optional (GL window focus)  ║
  ╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

Data source (same registry planned for CHANGE FOCUS):

  xyzfs/users/<uuid>/home/runtime/processes/
    processes.pdl
    processes/<pid>-<slug>/meta.pdl
      project_id, kind (app|widget|harness),
      launch_profile, session_path, gl_window 0|1,
      capabilities, display_name, heart.beat

Plus liveness:
  - kill(pid, 0) / /proc/<pid> check
  - GC dead entries (same as file-menu focus list)

FOCUS:
  - Writes "current focus" for this user/session so file-menu,
    tile-picker, map-picker default to that process
  - Does not require restarting those widgets if they poll focus

KILL vs SOFT QUIT:
  - SOFT: pieces/system/quit_request.txt or project quit_flag (house)
  - KILL: SIGTERM → wait → SIGKILL; then runtime_unregister / GC

Headless:
  - Still listed (harness sessions, NO_GL runs)
  - Marked no-window; FOCUS still valid for cmd-bus

Phases:
  PM1. Registry scan + list (read-only)
  PM2. REFRESH + GC dead pids
  PM3. FOCUS → shared focus file under xyzfs runtime
  PM4. SOFT QUIT + KILL
  PM5. GL chrome run-widget; optional raise window
  PM6. Harness: spawn editor headless, see in list, focus, kill, gone

KPIs:
  KPI-PM1: running editor appears within 2s of register
  KPI-PM2: dead pid GC removes row after kill
  KPI-PM3: FOCUS updates runtime/current_focus.txt (or equivalent)
  KPI-PM4: file-menu after FOCUS targets that process inbox
  KPI-PM5: KILL reaps process and registry entry
  KPI-PM6: headless session listed with gl=0 marker

Depends on: runtime register/unregister (file-menu roadmap §2).
Can ship list+kill on a harness-only registry before full GL.

'''

## Assistant

## Proc-monitor added to the plan (docs only)

### What it is
**`&.widgits/proc-monitor/`** — house task manager:

- Lists **registered** processes (apps, widgets, headless harnesses)
- **FOCUS** → other widgets (file-menu, etc.) target that process  
- **SOFT QUIT** / **KILL**  
- **REFRESH** + GC dead PIDs  
- GL primary; headless rows still show (`gl=0`)

Same **xyzfs runtime registry** as CHANGE FOCUS (not raw `ps` cosplay).

### Where documented
| Place | What |
|--------|------|
| `&.widgits/WIDGETS_ROADMAP.txt` **§5b** | Full UI, phases PM1–PM6, KPIs |
| `&.widgits/proc-monitor/README.txt` | Stub package |
| `&.widgits/README-widgets.txt` | Listed with other widgets |
| `%.harnesses/muta-zoo.md` **§12** | Pointer |
| `❤️‍🔥.XYZOS_README.md` | Widgets table + roadmap line |

### Depends on
Runtime **register / unregister** (still planned for editor/mutaclysm/widgets). Proc-monitor is the UI on top of that list.

No coding yet beyond docs — say when you want PM1 (list + GC) implemented.

- Execute: # Rewrite list/focus/kill to use proper PDL key match - embed function instead of wrong strstr
python3 << 'PY'
from pathlib import Path

pdl_fn = r'''
static void read_kv(const char *path, const char *key, char *out, size_t out_sz) {
    out[0] = '\0';
    FILE *f = fopen(path, "r");
    if (!f) return;
    char line[2048];
    while (fgets(line, sizeof(line), f)) {
        if (strncmp(line, "STATE", 5) != 0) continue;
        char *p1 = strchr(line, '|');
        if (!p1) continue;
        char *p2 = strchr(p1 + 1, '|');
        if (!p2) continue;
        *p2 = '\0';
        char *k = p1 + 1;
        while (*k == ' ' || *k == '\t') k++;
        char *ke = k + strlen(k) - 1;
        while (ke > k && (*ke == ' ' || *ke == '\t')) *ke-- = '\0';
        if (strcmp(k, key) != 0) continue;
        char *v = p2 + 1;
        while (*v == ' ' || *v == '\t') v++;
        v[strcspn(v, "\r\n")] = '\0';
        char *ve = v + strlen(v) - 1;
        while (ve > v && (*ve == ' ' || *ve == '\t')) *ve-- = '\0';
        snprintf(out, out_sz, "%s", v);
        break;
    }
    fclose(f);
}
'''

for name in ["runtime_list.c", "runtime_focus.c", "runtime_kill.c"]:
    p = Path("&.widgits/proc-monitor/ops") / name
    t = p.read_text()
    # replace old read_kv function - from static void read_kv to closing brace before next static/int
    import re
    t2 = re.sub(
        r'static void read_kv\(const char \*path, const char \*key, char \*out, size_t out_sz\) \{.*?\n\}',
        pdl_fn.strip(),
        t,
        count=1,
        flags=re.S,
    )
    if t2 == t:
        print("WARN no replace", name)
    else:
        p.write_text(t2)
        print("fixed", name)
PY
 (Fix PDL key parsing in proc-monitor ops)

#!/bin/bash
set -u
SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
HOUSE="$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/../.." && pwd)"
ACTION="${1:-help}"
PM="$HOUSE/&.widgits/proc-monitor"
case "$ACTION" in
    compile|c|build)
        bash "$PM/button.sh" compile
        ;;
    demo)
        bash "$0" compile || exit 1
        bash "$SCRIPT_DIR/scenarios/demo_register_focus_kill.sh"
        ;;
    help|*) echo "proc-monitor harness: compile | demo"; echo "PM=$PM" ;;
esac
EOF

cat > '%.harnesses/proc-monitor/scenarios/demo_register_focus_kill.sh' << 'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
set -u
HARNESS="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)"
HOUSE="$(cd "$HARNESS/../.." && pwd)"
PM="$HOUSE/&.widgits/proc-monitor"
OPS="$PM/ops/+x"
PROOF="$HARNESS/proof/harness-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)"
WD="$HARNESS/workdir/run-$$"
RUNTIME="$WD/runtime"
mkdir -p "$PROOF" "$RUNTIME" "$WD/fake_sess/pieces/system" "$WD/fake_sess2/pieces/system"
FAIL=0
pass(){ echo "PASS: $1"; }
fail(){ echo "FAIL: $1"; FAIL=1; }
cleanup() {
  [ -n "${PID1:-}" ] && kill -9 "$PID1" 2>/dev/null || true
  [ -n "${PID2:-}" ] && kill -9 "$PID2" 2>/dev/null || true
  rm -rf "$WD" 2>/dev/null || true
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "=== proc-monitor demo ==="
sleep 120 &
PID1=$!
sleep 120 &
PID2=$!

"$OPS/runtime_register.+x" "$RUNTIME" "$PID1" mutaclysm "$WD/fake_sess" app 1 "mutaclysm-demo" | tee "$PROOF/01_reg1.txt"
"$OPS/runtime_register.+x" "$RUNTIME" "$PID2" agy-editor "$WD/fake_sess2" app 0 "editor-headless" | tee "$PROOF/01_reg2.txt"

"$OPS/runtime_list.+x" "$RUNTIME" | tee "$PROOF/02_list.txt"
grep -q "pid=$PID1" "$PROOF/02_list.txt" && grep -q "pid=$PID2" "$PROOF/02_list.txt" && pass "list shows both pids" || fail "list missing pids"
grep -q "alive=1" "$PROOF/02_list.txt" && pass "pids reported alive" || fail "alive flags"

"$OPS/runtime_focus.+x" "$RUNTIME" "$PID1" | tee "$PROOF/03_focus.txt"
cp "$RUNTIME/current_focus.txt" "$PROOF/03_current_focus.txt"
grep -q "pid=$PID1" "$RUNTIME/current_focus.txt" && grep -q "project_id=mutaclysm" "$RUNTIME/current_focus.txt" && pass "FOCUS set to mutaclysm pid" || fail "focus file bad"

"$OPS/runtime_kill.+x" "$RUNTIME" "$PID1" --soft | tee "$PROOF/04_soft.txt"
[ -f "$WD/fake_sess/pieces/system/quit_request.txt" ] && pass "SOFT QUIT wrote quit_request.txt" || fail "no quit_request"

"$OPS/runtime_kill.+x" "$RUNTIME" "$PID2" | tee "$PROOF/05_kill.txt"
kill -9 "$PID2" 2>/dev/null || true
PID2=""
sleep 0.05
"$OPS/runtime_list.+x" "$RUNTIME" --gc | tee "$PROOF/06_list_gc.txt"
grep -q "pid=$PID2" "$PROOF/06_list_gc.txt" 2>/dev/null && fail "pid2 still listed" || pass "pid2 gone from registry after kill/gc"

kill -9 "$PID1" 2>/dev/null || true
PID1=""
"$OPS/runtime_list.+x" "$RUNTIME" --gc | tee "$PROOF/07_list_final.txt"

echo "Proof: $PROOF"
[ "$FAIL" -eq 0 ] && { echo "=== ALL PASS — proc-monitor ==="; exit 0; }
echo "=== FAILED ==="; exit 1
EOF
chmod +x '%.harnesses/proc-monitor/button.sh' '%.harnesses/proc-monitor/scenarios/demo_register_focus_kill.sh' '&.widgits/proc-monitor/button.sh'

cd '&.widgits/proc-monitor' && bash button.sh compile 2>&1 | tail -15
bash '%.harnesses/proc-monitor/scenarios/demo_register_focus_kill.sh' 2>&1

WD="$HARNESS/workdir/run-$$"
RUNTIME="$WD/runtime"
mkdir -p "$PROOF" "$RUNTIME" "$WD/fake_sess/pieces/system" "$WD/fake_sess2/pieces/system"
FAIL=0
pass(){ echo "PASS: $1"; }
fail(){ echo "FAIL: $1"; FAIL=1; }
cleanup() {
  [ -n "${PID1:-}" ] && kill -9 "$PID1" 2>/dev/null || true
  [ -n "${PID2:-}" ] && kill -9 "$PID2" 2>/dev/null || true
  rm -rf "$WD" 2>/dev/null || true
}
trap cleanup EXIT
echo "=== proc-monitor demo ==="
sleep 60 &
PID1=$!
sleep 60 &
PID2=$!
"$OPS/runtime_register.+x" "$RUNTIME" "$PID1" mutaclysm "$WD/fake_sess" app 1 "mutaclysm-demo"
"$OPS/runtime_register.+x" "$RUNTIME" "$PID2" agy-editor "$WD/fake_sess2" app 0 "editor-headless"
"$OPS/runtime_list.+x" "$RUNTIME" | tee "$PROOF/02_list.txt"
grep -q "pid=$PID1" "$PROOF/02_list.txt" && grep -q "pid=$PID2" "$PROOF/02_list.txt" && pass "list shows both pids" || fail "list"
grep -q "alive=1" "$PROOF/02_list.txt" && pass "alive" || fail "alive"
"$OPS/runtime_focus.+x" "$RUNTIME" "$PID1" | tee "$PROOF/03_focus.txt"
cp "$RUNTIME/current_focus.txt" "$PROOF/03_current_focus.txt"
grep -q "pid=$PID1" "$RUNTIME/current_focus.txt" && pass "FOCUS mutaclysm" || fail "focus"
"$OPS/runtime_kill.+x" "$RUNTIME" "$PID1" --soft | tee "$PROOF/04_soft.txt"
[ -f "$WD/fake_sess/pieces/system/quit_request.txt" ] && pass "SOFT quit_request" || fail "soft"
"$OPS/runtime_kill.+x" "$RUNTIME" "$PID2" | tee "$PROOF/05_kill.txt"
kill -9 "$PID2" 2>/dev/null || true
PID2=""
"$OPS/runtime_list.+x" "$RUNTIME" --gc | tee "$PROOF/06_gc.txt"
grep -q "editor-headless" "$PROOF/06_gc.txt" && fail "editor still listed" || pass "editor gone after kill"
kill -9 "$PID1" 2>/dev/null || true
PID1=""
"$OPS/runtime_list.+x" "$RUNTIME" --gc | tee "$PROOF/07_final.txt"
echo "Proof: $PROOF"
[ "$FAIL" -eq 0 ] && { echo "=== ALL PASS — proc-monitor ==="; exit 0; }
echo "=== FAILED ==="; exit 1
''')
demo.chmod(0o755)
r = subprocess.run(['bash', str(demo)], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=45)
print(r.stdout)
print(r.stderr)
print('exit', r.returncode)

- Execute: # Safe cleanup: only kill sleep children that look like harness leftovers (by cwd if possible)
# Do NOT use pkill -f on patterns that match this script
python3 << 'PY'
import os, signal, time
from pathlib import Path
killed = []
for pid_dir in Path('/proc').iterdir():
    if not pid_dir.name.isdigit():
        continue
    try:
        cmdline = (pid_dir / 'cmdline').read_bytes().replace(b'\x00', b' ').decode('utf-8', 'replace')
        # only pure sleep N where N is large harness sleeps
        parts = cmdline.strip().split()
        if len(parts) >= 2 and parts[0].endswith('sleep') and parts[1].isdigit() and int(parts[1]) >= 60:
            pid = int(pid_dir.name)
            if pid == os.getpid():
                continue
            os.kill(pid, signal.SIGTERM)
            killed.append((pid, cmdline[:80]))
    except (ProcessLookupError, PermissionError, FileNotFoundError):
        pass
print('killed harness sleeps:', killed)

## Assistant

**Not a bug — it was deliberately deferred.** Paint itself *is* done; **mouse → cell** is the missing front-end layer.

### What *is* done
The hard part of “paint a tile” is live:

- brush: `SET_BRUSH`
- write: `PLACE_TILE:map:x:y:glyph` → mutates `map.txt`
- harness: `%.harnesses/tile-picker+mutaclysm` **ALL PASS**

So the maker loop works as **coords / cmd**, not as “click the map.”

### Why click-on-map wasn’t in that pass

Roadmap order was explicit:

> **cmd first, GL click second**  
> (`WIDGETS_ROADMAP` KPI-T4, `muta-zoo.md`, `GL_CHROME_WIDGETS_PROPOSAL`)

Click paint needs more than `muta_place_tile`:

1. **Hit-test on the mutaclysm map**  
   Pixel/mouse → `(map_id, x, y)` (camera, cell size, GL mirror, not just ASCII).

2. **GL chrome / input path**  
   Widgets are still **ops-only**. Full GL chrome is proposal-only. Without a real widget window + input path, there’s no product place to own “I’m in paint mode.”

3. **Cross-window focus**  
   Ideal UX is: brush in tile-picker, **click cells in mutaclysm**. That’s two windows, one keyboard/mouse focus problem. Proposal calls that **Phase D** (shared focus / coords file / Xdnd-style). Easier interim: **place at xlector** (cursor already in the game) — also not wired as the default product path yet.

4. **Session priority**  
   We finished the shared spine first: file-menu bus, muta save/load, map switch, place-by-coords, proc-monitor. Click paint sits **on top** of that spine; without place-by-coords, click would be untestable.

### Bottom line

| Layer | Status |
|--------|--------|
| Write cell on map | Done |
| Harness / cmd bus | Done |
| Click map → place brush | **Not started** (depends on GL hit-test + widget chrome / xlector shortcut) |

So it’s unfinished **product UX**, not unfinished **map mutation**. Next slice is either:

- **A (smaller):** PLACE at **current xlector** from tile-picker (no mouse on map), or  
- **B (real click):** mutaclysm mouse → cell → `PLACE_TILE` while brush is set.

Want that next?

## Assistant

Wrote investigation + design doc (no product code):

**`101.mutaclsym…/dox/xelector-context.md`**

### What it covers

1. **Live baseline** — xlector/`interact_mode`, craft-style `active_panel`, shared ASCII+GL input (`history` / interact_relay), Space free today, digit vs POV conflict when menu open.

2. **Context menu (Space in interact)** — `1 Event · 2 Copy · 3 Paste · 4 Delete · 5 Exit`, arrows + digits, Esc layered close — modeled on existing craft/inventory panels, not a second TTY.

3. **Event editor** — RMMV-like fields (name, trigger, sprite, switches) + script pane; **GL = blocks/buttons**, **ASCII = `.pal`**, with optional raw pal edit; both lower/raise from one IR.

4. **Disk / SAVE** — `world_01/<map>/events/ev_…/` + world switches so **existing full-tree save/load already persists events**.

5. **Runtime** — new `event_run.+x` interpreting pages; triggers start with **action** (Enter); blocks can `call_op` existing `.+x` ops.

6. **Phases A→F** — menu shell → clipboard → event files → runner → scratch L1 → RMMV breadth (so agents don’t boil the ocean).

7. **Open questions** with defaults (Space only in interact, Delete ≠ terrain, in-process view not widget, custom interpreter first).

### Anchors used
- Current `choice.c` / `move_player.c` / `compose_frame` panels  
- `#.wussup` maker/event-editor intent  
- RMMV catalog at `#.rmmv-events-explained`  
- `muta_world_io` save-tree behavior  

When you want coding, recommended first slice is **Phase A** (Space menu shell + harness) only.

## Assistant

Got it — architecture is **widget + desktop drop**, not an in-process mutaclysm editor. That’s locked in and the first slice is live.

### Architecture (your model)
```text
pickers / muta Event  →  #.desktop/ tray  →  event-editor widget (independent)
                              │
                              └── drop/import → mutaclysm world
```
- **Event editor** = `&.widgits/event-editor/` (own process; can run alone)
- Edits **desktop packages** (events, and later pets/charas)
- **Pickers place onto desktop** (`tp_place_desktop`)
- Mutaclysm only: Space menu → Event exports package + open request

### Built now

| Piece | What |
|--------|------|
| `#.desktop/` | House tray: `events/ entities/ tiles/ inbox/` |
| `&.widgits/event-editor/` | Ops: resolve desktop, package init, open request, import to world, export entity |
| `tp_place_desktop` | Tile brush → `#.desktop/tiles/` |
| Mutaclysm Space menu | Only in **interact**; at xlector cell; 1 Event · 2 Copy · 3 Paste · 4 Delete · 5 Exit |
| Event | Creates desktop package + `inbox/event_editor_open.request` |
| Delete | Clears **glyph at cell** (content at space) |
| Docs | `dox/xelector-context.md`, `ctrl-legend.md`, widget README/plan |

### Harness
```bash
bash '%.harnesses/event-editor+desktop/button.sh' demo
# === ALL PASS — event-editor + desktop ===
```

### Live play (muta)
1. Enter interact (xlector)
2. Move cursor
3. **Space** → menu
4. **1** Event → package on `#.desktop/events/` + open request for the widget

- GL `run-widget` chrome for event-editor

## Assistant

**Updated GL mock is open** — RMMV layout kept, with **CHTPM nav** and a **Commands / Scratch** toggle.

### What’s new in the window
| House standard | In GL mock |
|----------------|------------|
| `[>]` / `[ ]` focus | Method bar, pages, fields, contents, footer |
| Numbered rows + **KEY:n** | Digits 1–9 jump/fire in current zone |
| **Enter** commit | Sets Active mode (CHTPM-style) |
| **Esc** BACK | Active → Nav → Methods zone → quit |
| Status line | `Nav > _` / `Active > _` + zone + view |
| Method strip | Like `game.chtpm` `${piece_methods}` / `onClick=KEY:n` |

### Commands ↔ Scratch
- **Tab** or **method 5 / KEY:5** toggles  
- **Commands*** = RMMV Contents list  
- **Scratch*** = same graph as nested blocks (`[ when ]` / `{ show_text }`)  
- Both are the dual-source story (`.pal` / IR later)

### Keys
```
arrows      move [>] in zone
Left/Right  change zone (or along method/footer bar)
PgUp/PgDn   cycle zones
1-9         KEY:n
Enter       commit
Tab / 5     Commands | Scratch
Esc         back layers
q           quit
```

### Files
